# Fabricated: fixed findings

Every defect found and fixed, by ID: where it was, how it failed and what triggered it, why the tests missed it, and
the fix with how it was verified. Open findings are in [`roadmap.md`](roadmap.md#open-findings); a finding moves here, in
the same change, once its fix is verified. Code, commits and the other documents cite findings by ID.

Severity: **P0** crash or data loss on a normal launch · **P1** a supported feature silently doesn't work ·
**P2** wrong behaviour or a leak under realistic conditions · **P3** diagnostics, hygiene, unlikely conditions.

| ID | Sev | Location | Failure mode and trigger | Why tests missed it | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OVERLOAD-3 | P1 on NeoForge 21.1 and Forge 52 with FrozenLib 2.2.4 (its bone meal API: the bone meal behaviours FrozenLib mods give blocks, Wilder Wild's among them, never ran); on every platform for an injector moved into the method the code moved to whose handler takes a local by its ordinal | `OverloadRepairs.localsInOverload` (an `@Local` taking its local by ordinal, read first), `vanillasOrdinalThere`, `passesItsParameters` (new), `PatchAlignment.vanillaPlaces` (new, out of `LocalRepairs.vanillaLocalRepairs`) | FrozenLib's (GPL-3.0; 2.2.4 for 1.21.1) `BoneMealItemMixin.frozenLib$runBonemeal` is an `@Inject` at the `BlockState.getBlock` call in `BoneMealItem.growCrop`, taking the method's parameters, the callback info and `@Local(ordinal = 0) BlockState`. NeoForge 21.1.265 and Forge 52.1.21 mark `growCrop` deprecated and keep it only to call `applyBonemeal`, which also takes the player, on a server level (read in their classes); `applyBonemeal` holds vanilla's code after the platform's bone meal event, whose result it keeps in a local of another type. The overload repairs find the replacement and the point there (DEPRECATED-1) and bridge the handler, but followed its `@Local`s into the other method only by name, or by type where one local of the type besides the inserted parameter is in scope; one taking its local by ordinal stopped the move, so pre-flight left the hook out ("captures the local variable of type net.minecraft.world.level.block.state.BlockState, which growCrop in net.minecraft.world.item.BoneMealItem does not declare"). Apoli's (MIT; 2.12.0-pre.4 in Origins 1.13.0-pre.4) bed-spawn and drop hooks, left out beside it on both platforms, don't share the cause: NeoForge runs `startSleepInBed`'s code in a lambda whose result its sleep event may overrule, and Apoli's `@WrapOperation` there takes MixinExtras' `@Cancellable`, which in the lambda would cancel the lambda alone (LAMBDA-2's move takes no sugar); `ServerPlayer.drop(boolean)` calls NeoForge's `CommonHooks.onPlayerTossEvent`, another class's method making the `drop` call Apoli's `@ModifyArg` and the `@Inject` sharing its state name. Additional Entity Attributes' (MIT) water speed constant, now in NeoForge's `sinkInFluid` taking the fluid type, would apply to every fluid there. | DEPRECATED-1's test hooked without sugar; no test moved an injector taking a local by its ordinal. | This change: an `@Local(ordinal = n)` of the handler of an injector moved into the method the code moved to (`OverloadRepairs.vanillasOrdinalThere`) takes vanilla's local of that ordinal, where vanilla's version of the method (VANILLA-1) is known and the method passes its own parameters on to the other with the one it inserts: shown by `insertedParameter` for a method doing nothing else, by `passesItsParameters` (each call of the replacement pushes the parameters in order, a constant at the inserted one) for a deprecated one; code split off into a method taking the same parameter types, which may be other values, stays out as before. Vanilla's code aligned with the other method's (`PatchAlignment`) must place each instruction the point picks in vanilla's method, with certainty, at the one it picks there, in order. At each, vanilla's locals of the type its local variable table has in scope are found there: a parameter in its parameter's place, another local by the store its scope starts after, placed with certainty, in scope at the point; they must be exactly the other method's locals of the type there that are vanilla's (`isVanillasLocal`; the inserted parameter is the platform's), and the `@Local` takes the ordinal the nth has among all of the type in scope there, as MixinExtras counts them, the same at each point. Where the running Mixin keeps locals past their scope (Forge's, PRE-26), those of the type it keeps below the local must be those in scope, a slot it may look up again after a frame told only where it is a parameter of another type, covered by its one entry. As Mixin's discriminator, an ordinal is read before a slot and a name. Anything that can't be told so leaves the hook out. FrozenLib's keeps ordinal 0 in `applyBonemeal`, so its behaviours run there, after the platform's bone meal event, as vanilla's growth does. The other hooks above are left as they are. `MixinPreflightTest.aLocalTakenByItsOrdinalMovesWithAnInjectionToTheMethodTheCodeMovedInto` (NeoForge's shape; an inserted parameter of the local's type; a platform local of the type before vanilla's and one after it, which fails where every local counted as vanilla's; vanilla's second; the inserted parameter between vanilla's; several points), `aLocalTakenByItsOrdinalStaysOutWhereVanillasLocalThereCantBeToldForSure` (a platform local made as vanilla's first, whose store the alignments disagree on, which the first fix took vanilla's second for; another call of the point; a replacement taking the parameters out of order; split-off code; no vanilla code), `aLocalTakenByItsOrdinalAndNameIsTakenByItsOrdinalAsMixinTakesIt`, `aLocalTakenByItsOrdinalIsCountedAmongTheLocalsForgesMixinKeepsPastTheirScope`. Verified: core's tests (675); NeoForge 1.21.1's and Forge 52's advanced corpus servers against runs before the change: "@Inject frozenLib$runBonemeal targets growCrop(...), which NeoForge deprecated for applyBonemeal(...); the injector is pointed there" (Forge's the same) and Mixin applies it, FrozenLib's other hooks, the Apoli and Additional Entity Attributes hooks above and the checks as before; their server parts as before. |
| PROVIDER-22 | P2 on NeoForge 21.1 with YUNG's API (every YUNG structure mod): its locate replacer's data pack was never offered, so the vanilla structures YUNG's mods replace or remove weren't in the `c:hidden_from_locator_selection` structure tag other mods' structure locators read; on 1.21.11 and 26.x the same for any mod's mixin of the class there | `ModResourcePackCreator` (1.21.1's `impl.resource.loader`; later versions' `impl.resource.pack`, from `common` to `common12111`), `ResourceLoaderBridge.offerBuiltinPacks` (1.21.1), `BuiltinPacks` (1.21.11, 26.x); the test mod's `ModResourcePackCreatorMixin` (1.21.1, `common12111`) | Upstream's `ModResourcePackCreator` (Apache-2.0; read in the `fabric-resource-loader` jars of Fabric API 0.116.17, 0.141.6, 0.155.3 and the 26.2 and 26.3 releases the plans name) is the pack finder upstream gives each pack repository: an instance keeps the pack type it was made for, made by a constructor taking the type and whether the repository is the client's of a server's known packs, and its `loadPacks` offers Fabric's mod packs and the built-in packs. Fabricated's native resource loader had the class for its pack source alone and offered the built-in packs from a lambda. YUNG's API's (LGPL-3.0; 5.1.9 for 1.21.1, 6.1.3 for 26.1.2, read in their jars) `ModResourcePackCreatorMixinFabric` shadows the pack type, makes its locate replacer's pack source at the end of that constructor where the type is server data, and offers its pack at the end of `loadPacks`: a required built-in data pack holding the tag, appending the structures its mods' locate replacements and removals name. Pre-flight left all three out on 1.21.1 ("@Shadow field type ... does not exist", "@Inject targets method <init>(Lnet/minecraft/server/packs/PackType;Z)V, which does not exist", the same for `loadPacks`). `/locate`'s redirection to YUNG's own structures is its `LocateCommandMixin`, `ServerLevelMixin` and `ChunkGeneratorMixin`, which applied. Upstream's class has the same instance shape on 1.21.11 and 26.x, where YUNG's 26.1.2 build targets it, and Fabricated's had the same gap. | No test mod or fixture hooked a Fabric API internal class Fabricated's native modules replace. | This change: each version's `ModResourcePackCreator` has the part of upstream's instance shape those hooks use: the pack type, a constructor taking it and the known-packs flag, and `loadPacks`, which offers the built-in packs as before. Upstream's public constructor taking the type alone, its `activationInfo` and flag fields (`forClientDataPackManager`, from 1.21.11 `forKnownPacksManager`), `CLIENT_RESOURCE_PACK_PROVIDER` and `registerModPack` aren't there, so a mixin of those is still left out. The pack finder each pack repository gets through NeoForge's `AddPackFindersEvent` is one, made with the event's pack type and whether the repository is NeoForge's trusted one (the client's of a server's known packs, upstream's flag). It doesn't offer Fabric's mod packs, which NeoForge builds itself. The 1.21.1 index of native module packages loses `impl/resource/pack`, which 1.21.1's in-game mod no longer has. The test mod's `ModResourcePackCreatorMixin` has YUNG's shape (`pack-creator-hooks`, every NeoForge version, every server run; the report must not leave the mixin out): on 1.21.1 a data pack it offers at the end of `loadPacks` must be among the server's packs; from 1.21.11 (`common12111`'s half of the test mod, whose mixin config `mc/platform-neoforge.gradle` now finds in a version's shared folders too) its hook there must run for the server's creator, whose built-in data pack the server has. Verified: NeoForge 1.21.1, 1.21.11 and 26.1.2 build; their server parts pass every check, `pack-creator-hooks` with the test mods, with Fabric API, with stand-ins and beside Forgified Fabric API, 26.1.2's otherwise as before; on NeoForge 1.21.1's advanced corpus server, against a run before the change, YUNG's three hooks apply and the server finds its pack ("Found new data pack mod/yungsapi:locate_replacer, loading it automatically"), nothing else in the report changes, and the checks are as before; pre-flight finds nothing in YUNG's 26.1.2 build's mixin against NeoForge 26.1.2's class. 26.2 and 26.3 don't build locally. |
| SEAM-7 | P1 on Forge 47 with Porting Lib (Create, Farmer's Delight Refabricated: every Fabric mod's hook of vanilla's removal in the server's and the client's `destroyBlock` left out, in the corpus runs); on every platform where a Fabric mod's interface brings a second default of the extension's `onDestroyedByPlayer` or `canHarvestBlock` to the block state or the block | `KeepsDefault.declares`, `DefaultBridge.method` (moved from `FabricClassTransforms`, now synthetic) | Where a Fabric mod's interface on a game class brings a default the class already has from the platform's interface, Fabricated declares the method there, calling the platform's default (CORPUS-11): Porting Lib's `BlockStateExtensions` brings `onDestroyedByPlayer(Level, BlockPos, Player, boolean, FluidState)` to Forge 47's `BlockState`, beside `IForgeBlockState`'s. SEAM-3's check of whether a block keeps the platform's default asks whether the state's class declares the method, and counted that bridge as an override: every block took Forge's path, without the hooks of vanilla's removal (the test mod's calcite broke to air, its hook of `removeBlock` running 0 times). The class was put back as pre-flight said, the test mod's wrapper in it; only the check's answer differed. On Fabric, vanilla's `destroyBlock` calls `removeBlock` itself and the hook runs. | The fixtures had no default bridge on the state or the block, and the plain server parts no mod bringing one; the corpus run's check is informational. | This change: the bridges are synthetic (not `ACC_BRIDGE`), as a method no source declares, and the check doesn't count a synthetic method that isn't a compiler's bridge, asking the class's interfaces as it would without it; a mod's own declaration still overrides. The bridge's code moved to core as it was. `VanillaCallsTest.aDefaultBridgeKeepsThePlatformsDefault` (every platform's form: a mod's interfaces bringing both extensions' methods to the state and the block, bridged; the hooks of the removal and of the harvest check run, the breaks as before, overriding blocks and states still overriding), failing before. Forge 47's server part, and its corpus server: `vanilla block breaking OK`. |
| SEAM-6 | P1 on NeoForge 21.1 to 26.3 and Forge 47 to 66 for BCLib's shears hooks and Porting Lib's tool action hooks, and on Forge 66 for Fabric API's modded fishing rods hook (left out, or wrapping another check); P2 on the 1.20.1 and 1.21.1 client for Apoli's elytra power; for any Fabric mod hooking vanilla's check of a stack's item where the platform asks the stack's actions | `VanillaCalls` (rows of `Code.ACTION`, `FORGE_ACTION` and `LAYER`, `ItemCheck`, `Holder`, `actions`, `itemCheck`, `layerBase`, `mayApply`; `NOT_PUT_BACK` with each row's evidence), `KeepsDefault.asked`, `SeamConflicts` (`Candidate`, `Read`), `VanillaSeams` (`mayChange`, the view's prefilter), `MixinPreflight.isSeamNote`, `KnownLimits`; the test mod's `ShearsCheckMixin` and `VanillaItemCheckTest` | Vanilla asks `stack.is(Items.SHEARS)` in `BeehiveBlock.useItemOn` (1.20.1's `use`), `PumpkinBlock.useItemOn`, `TripWireBlock.playerWillDestroy` and the `mobInteract` of `Sheep`, `SnowGolem`, `MushroomCow` and `Bogged`; `stack.is(Items.FISHING_ROD)` in `FishingHook.shouldStopFishing` (twice) and the client's `FishingHookRenderer` (1.20.1's `render`, 1.21.1's `getPlayerHandPos`); up to 1.21.1 `stack.is(Items.SHIELD)` in `Player.hurtCurrentlyUsedShield` and the piglin's `StopHoldingItemIfNoLongerAdmiring` lambda; and on the client `stack.is(Items.ELYTRA)` in `ElytraLayer.render` (1.20.1, 1.21.1; read in vanilla 1.21.1's and 1.21.11's jars through Mojang's mappings, and in 26.1.2's, 26.2's and 26.3's, where `is(Item)` is `TypedInstance.is(T)`, called as `is(Object)`). NeoForge 21.1.265, 21.11.45 and 26.1.2.116 and Forge 47.4.28 (under SRG's names), 52.1.21, 61.2.4, 64.1.5, 65.1.5 and 66.1.0 ask the stack's `canPerformAction(ItemAbilities.X)` or `canPerformAction(ToolActions.X)` there instead (`SHEARS_HARVEST`, `SHEARS_CARVE`, `SHEARS_DISARM`, `FISHING_ROD_CAST`, `SHIELD_BLOCK`), the mobs' only on Forge 65 and 66 (read in their classes; NeoForge 26.2.0.90 and 26.3.0.95 in their patches). NeoForge, and Forge 47 to 64, compile the mobs' shears check out (`if (false && ...)`, NeoForge's "Shear logic is handled by IShearable"): the shears item shears a mob through `IShearable` or `IForgeShearable`, which posts the platforms' events and drops what their loot says; NeoForge 21.11 on keeps the mooshroom's. The stacks' defaults (`IItemStackExtension`, on 26.x `ItemInstanceExtension`, and `IForgeItemStack`) ask the item's (`IItemExtension`, `IForgeItem`), which answers no but for a sword's sweep and dig and a pickaxe's dig (Forge 61 to 65's `DEFAULT_SWORD_ACTIONS` and `DEFAULT_PICKAXE_ACTIONS`, read in `ToolActions`), a sword's sweep (NeoForge 21.11 on, Forge 66) and a campfire's dousing (NeoForge 26.3); vanilla's `ShearsItem`, `FishingRodItem` and, where a shield action is asked, `ShieldItem` override it with their actions. The elytra layer asks `this.shouldRender(stack, entity)` (NeoForge 21.1 and Forge 47 declare it on the layer, Forge 52 on `IForgeElytraLayer`), whose default is `stack.getItem() == Items.ELYTRA`. The held item renderer asks `getItem() instanceof MapItem` and `instanceof CrossbowItem` (NeoForge 21.1 to 26.1.2; Forge the crossbow's), and the item frame renderer (NeoForge 21.1, Forge 47 and 52) `instanceof MapItem`, where vanilla asks for its own map and crossbow. So pre-flight left out BCLib's (MIT, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 for some assets; 21.0.13 for 1.21.1, 26.101.5 and 26.302.1) `shears.PumpkinBlockMixin`, `TripWireBlockMixin`, `SheepMixin` and `SnowGolemMixin` (each a `@WrapOperation` of `is(Item)`, answering yes for the items of the common shears tag), while its `BeehiveBlockMixin` and `MushroomCowMixin` wrapped the glass bottle's and the bowl's checks alone; Porting Lib's (LGPL; tool actions 2.3.12 to 2.3.15 in Create 6.0.8.1 for 1.20.1) `BeehiveBlockMixin`, `PumpkinBlockMixin`, `TripWireBlockMixin`, `FishingHookMixin`, `PlayerMixin.shieldToolAction`, `StopHoldingItemIfNoLongerAdmiringMixin` and, on the client, `FishingHookRendererMixin` (its own tool actions asked beside vanilla's check); Apoli's (MIT; 2.9.2 in Origins 1.10.2 for 1.20.1, 2.12.0-pre.4 in Origins 1.13.0-pre.4 for 1.21.1) elytra layer hooks (`ElytraFeatureRendererMixin`'s `@Redirect`, `ElytraFlightPowerTypeMixin`'s `@ModifyExpressionValue`), and on the client Apoli 2.12's `HeldItemRendererMixin` and Porting Lib base's `ItemFrameRendererMixin`; on Forge 66, Fabric API's (Apache-2.0; 0.164.0 for 26.3) `fabric-item-api-v1` `FishingHookMixin.allowModdedRods` (a `@WrapOperation` of `is(Object)` in `shouldStopFishing`, answering yes for any `FishingRodItem`). BCLib 3.0.17 (1.20.1) hooks the methods' starts and `ItemStack.is` itself, which apply, though Forge 47's code there asks `canPerformAction`. The other mods of the corpora read for these calls find vanilla's: Debugify's pumpkin overlay hook (LGPL-3.0), Moonlight's (Supplementaries Team License 1.5 for 1.21.1 and 26.1.2) and Supplementaries' (the same licence) cartography table hooks; FrozenLib's (GPL-3.0) hooks `ItemStack.is` itself. What the hooks do didn't happen: the common shears tag's items harvesting a beehive, carving a pumpkin, disarming a tripwire or shearing a mob; the tool actions of Create's Porting Lib in vanilla's places; a modded fishing rod keeping its line on Forge 66; Origins' elytra power drawing its wings. | No fixture or test-mod hook named a vanilla item check the platforms ask through an item extension. | This change: `VanillaCalls` gets rows of a third shape. Where a Fabric mod's hook names vanilla's check there, the stack the platform's code pushes is kept and its classes asked whether they keep both extensions' defaults (`KeepsDefault`, as SEAM-3 asks of a block state and its block, the answers kept per class); then vanilla's `stack.is(Items.X)` is made, which hooks find, or the action and the platform's call as before. The stack's code runs once and the method gets no local, so a hook capturing a local (Porting Lib's line hook) finds vanilla's. For an item keeping the default both answer no, and an item overriding it is asked as the platform has it, without the hook. The check is `is(Item)` (`m_150930_` under SRG's names), or in Java 25's classes (26.1 on) `is(Object)`; on NeoForge 26.x the stack's extension is `ItemInstanceExtension`, its item's taking an `ItemInstance`. The elytra layer's check goes back for the layers whose class keeps `shouldRender` (`KeepsDefault.keepsMethod` from the class or interface declaring it). Pre-flight notes each hook ("@WrapOperation fabricatedTest$shears hooks ItemStack.is(Lnet/minecraft/world/item/Item;)Z in playerWillDestroy, vanilla's call NeoForge's TripWireBlock makes through the item's IItemExtension.canPerformAction instead; Fabricated puts it back there for the items keeping that method's default, so the hook applies to their check, and an item overriding it (vanilla's shears among them) is checked as NeoForge has it, without the hook (SEAM-6)"), and a mixin left out takes its SEAM-6 notes with it. A native mod's mixin of the `canPerformAction` call, of the extensions' `canPerformAction`, of `ItemStack.is` or `TypedInstance.is`, or of the layer's `shouldRender` keeps the class as the platform has it. A hook of a mob's shears check where the platform has none, and of the held item and item frame renderers' checks, stays out, its hint saying why ("Sheep's mobInteract asks for no shears here: the platform shears it from the shears item, through its shearable extension (NeoForge's IShearable, Forge's IForgeShearable), which posts its events and drops what its loot says, so Fabricated doesn't put vanilla's check back there (SEAM-6)."), with a known-limit line: putting the check back would shear past the platforms' events. The seam conflict check read every native mixin again for each seam class: it now reads each once for all of them, parses only one naming a class concerned, and works out a class's rewrite only where such a mixin targets it or selects a method a put-back there may change. Pre-flight's view doesn't parse a seam class whose bytes name none of its rows' platform calls and actions (`Player` from 1.21.5 on). The checks the put-backs copy into a class read the answers kept for the classes first and make nothing (36 bytes a check before, measured; SEAM-3's and SEAM-4's too), as the item checks run every tick and every frame. `ItemChecksTest` (each platform's form, NeoForge 21.x's and 26.x's, Forge's and Forge 47's under SRG's names, for an empty hand, plain items, vanilla's shears, rod and shield and a platform mod's tools overriding `canPerformAction` either way: the rewritten classes do what the platform's did for each, a hook runs for the plain ones alone; the elytra layer, its own and Forge 52's interface's, and a layer overriding it; the answers kept per class across threads; classes put back in parallel the same; what a native mixin conflicts on; each native mixin read once, five times before with five seam classes; 200,000 checks making under 4 KB, 7.2 MB before), `RestoredSeamsTest` (the note on NeoForge and Forge 47, the hints and limits); the test mod's `vanilla-items` check (NeoForge, every run; Forge, with Fabric API). Verified: core's tests (659) and CI's script tests (62); the inventory; NeoForge 1.21.1 and 26.1.2 and Forge 47, 52 and 66 build. Local servers, every check passing: NeoForge 1.21.1 (server 85, with Fabric API 93, stand-ins 91, with Forgified Fabric API 78) and 26.1.2 (87, 92, 93, 78), Forge 47 (111, 125, 119, with Forgified Fabric API 108), 52 (108, 125, 114) and 66 (99, 115, 106); `vanilla-items` passes in each, beside Forgified Fabric API too. The corpus servers against runs before the change (NeoForge 1.21.1, Forge 52 and Forge 66 advanced, Forge 47's corpus): BCLib's `PumpkinBlockMixin` and `TripWireBlockMixin` apply on all three, with its `BeehiveBlockMixin` noted at the shears check, and on Forge 66 its sheep, snow golem and mooshroom hooks and Fabric API's `FishingHookMixin`; on NeoForge 1.21.1 and Forge 52 its sheep and snow golem hooks stay out with the new hint and limit; on Forge 47 Porting Lib's six tool action hooks apply; the reports' other changes are the test mod's notes, and the checks are as before (each run's failing informational checks fail on the run before the change too). The client rows are checked by unit tests only. Discovery on NeoForge 26.1.2's server with Fabric API (the bench's run) allocates 269 to 271 MB, from 265 to 267 MB on `main`: what the rows cost, about 4 MB, is spread over the launch, no step of it more than JFR's sampling tells apart. Review fixes: the bogged's shears check on Forge 65 and 66, which asks the action as the other mobs' do, had no row, so a hook of it was left out with no hint; Forge's held item hint named the map check, which only NeoForge changes. |
| SEAM-5 | P2 on Forge 47 with a Forge mod's mixin of a class SEAM-1, SEAM-3 or SEAM-4 puts vanilla's code back in (the put-back left out there) | `SeamConflicts` (each mixin's strings read through its refmap), `InstalledClasses` (`Listing.refmaps`, `RefmapFile`, `ListedMixin.refmap`), `NativeModMixins.listed` | Forge 47 runs the game under SRG's names, and its mods are built with Mojang's: their mixins name the game's methods by those, which their configs' refmaps map to SRG's (Mixin reads the refmap's `mappings`). The seam conflict check read a native mixin's `method` and `@At` strings as written, so a Mojang name it couldn't find in the class counted as what can't be told: a Forge 47 mod's mixin of any method of `PistonStructureResolver`, the server's game mode or `BlockBehaviour` kept the class as Forge has it, and Fabric mods' hooks of the seams there found nothing, the report naming the mixin as hooking Forge's code there. Found in SEAM-4's review. | The tests' native mixins named the methods by the names the game runs with, and the runs' only native mixins on Forge 47, Forgified Fabric API's, do hook Forge's code there. | This change: a native mixin's config's refmap goes with its mixins, read from the jar the first time a mixin that may concern a seam class is checked, and once; the mixin's annotations' strings are read as the refmap maps them, and as written. A refmap the jar hasn't, or that doesn't parse, maps nothing, as Mixin then reads the strings as they are; one that fails to read keeps the class, unread, and the decision isn't cached. `SeamConflictsTest.aNativeMixinsStringsAreReadThroughItsRefmap` (Forge 47's classes under SRG's names: a mixin at `destroyBlock`'s start keeps the class unmapped and lets it be put back mapped; one at vanilla's harvest check the rewrite adds, mapped, keeps it), `NativeModMixinsTest.aConfigsRefmapIsReadOnlyWhereAsked`. |
| SEAM-4 | P1 on NeoForge 21.1 to 26.3 and Forge 47 to 66 for Apoli's harvest power and, through SHARE-2, its block-break action, and Porting Lib's harvestable blocks hook (left out); for any Fabric mod wrapping vanilla's harvest check in the server's `destroyBlock` or in `getDestroyProgress` | `VanillaCalls` (four rows, `Code.HARVEST`, `FORGE_HARVEST`, `PROGRESS` and `FORGE_PROGRESS`; `NOT_PUT_BACK`; `copied`, `resolves`), `KeepsDefault.keepsMethod`, `VanillaSeams` (`findings`, the view's member index), `SeamConflicts` (each class's other classes), `MixinPreflight.noteRestoredSeams` (each row's note), `CallRepairs.putBackOrdinalRepairs` (places by call), `KnownLimits`; the test mod's `HarvestCheckMixin`, `DestroyProgressMixin` and `VanillaHarvestTest`, `BreakOverride.canHarvestBlock` and the platforms' `TestPlatform.grantHarvest` | Vanilla's server `destroyBlock` asks `this.player.hasCorrectToolForDrops(state)` (a `ServerPlayer` call) whether the break drops anything, and `BlockBehaviour.getDestroyProgress` asks `player.hasCorrectToolForDrops(state)` for its divisor (30 or 100). NeoForge 21.1.265, 21.11.45 and 26.1.2.116 and Forge 47.4.28 (under SRG's names), 52.1.21, 61.2.4, 64.1.5, 65.1.5 and 66.1.0 ask `state.canHarvestBlock(level, pos, player)` in the first and a hook of theirs in the second, `EventHooks.doPlayerHarvestCheck(player, state, level, pos)` and `ForgeHooks.isCorrectToolForDrops(state, player)` (read in their classes; NeoForge 26.2.0.90 and 26.3.0.95 in their patches). NeoForge's defaults (`IBlockStateExtension` to `IBlockExtension` to `doPlayerHarvestCheck`, compiled the same in all five) make vanilla's call and post `PlayerEvent.HarvestCheck` with its answer. Forge's (`IForgeBlock` to `isCorrectToolForDrops`) asks `player.hasCorrectToolForDrops(state)`, which Forge makes fire its `HarvestCheck` with vanilla's answer, where the state needs the correct tool, and fires the event with `true` where it doesn't, as Forge's `Player.hasCorrectToolForDrops` does there; Forge's `BreakEvent` asks `isCorrectToolForDrops` too, for its experience. In the same `destroyBlock` both platforms ask the held item's `canAttackBlock` (1.21.11 on: the stack's `canDestroyBlock`) inside their block break event's hook (`CommonHooks.fireBlockBreak`, `ForgeHooks.onBlockBreakEvent`), which pre-cancels the event where the item says no and lets a listener overrule it, where vanilla returns first; NeoForge 26.1.2 on asks it there on the client too (its own check compiled out, `if (false && ...)`), which NeoForge 21.1 and 21.11 and Forge keep. NeoForge's `DoorBlock.playerWillDestroy` asks its own overload, `hasCorrectToolForDrops(state, level, pos)`. The platforms' `getDestroySpeed(state)` call in `getDestroyProgress` takes the position (`getDigSpeed` on NeoForge 21.1 and Forge 47), which the overload and replacement repairs already move hooks to. So pre-flight left out Apoli's (MIT; 2.12.0-pre.4 in Origins 1.13.0-pre.4 for 1.21.1) `ModifyHarvestPowerTypeMixin$HarvestabilityProxy.apoli$modifyHarvest` (a `@WrapOperation` of the server's `ServerPlayer.hasCorrectToolForDrops`), with its `apoli$cacheBreakingBlock` (SHARE-1) and `ServerPlayerInteractionManagerMixin.apoli$actionOnBlockBreak` (SHARE-2), and its `$BlockBreakingDeltaProxy` (the same call in `getDestroyProgress`); and Porting Lib's (LGPL; 2.3.12 to 2.3.15 for 1.20.1, in Create) `port_lib$canHarvestBlock`, which asks its `HarvestableBlock`, its `@ModifyExpressionValue port_lib$blockBreakHook` of `canAttackBlock` (its own break event, its `port_lib$popXp` going with it), while its entity module's `@Inject` at `getDestroyProgress`'s call was pointed at Forge's hook (HOOK-3). Apoli 2.9.2 (MIT, 1.20.1) hooks `Player.hasCorrectToolForDrops` itself and a variable after the call, which apply. The other mods of the corpora read for these calls (BetterEnd, MIT with CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 assets; Wilder Wild, LGPL-3.0 for 3.0.7 and FrozenBlock Modding Oasis License 1.0 for 4.x; Axiom, All Rights Reserved; Artifacts, MIT; Carpet, MIT; Polymer, LGPL-3.0) make them in their own code or hook other methods. What the hooks do (an Origins power letting a player harvest or mine what vanilla's check refuses, and its block-break actions; Create's blocks' harvest rules) didn't happen. | No fixture or test-mod hook named a vanilla call the platforms make through a static hook of theirs, nor the harvest check: SEAM-3's fixture stood a plain method in for it. | This change: `VanillaCalls` gets four rows. Where a Fabric mod's hook names `hasCorrectToolForDrops` there, the call goes back in front of the platform's, in a branch taken only where the platform's default makes it the same way: in `destroyBlock` for the blocks whose state's and block's classes keep `canHarvestBlock`'s default (asked as SEAM-3 asks, the answers kept apart for each extension method), and on Forge for the players whose class keeps `Player.hasCorrectToolForDrops` (`KeepsDefault.keepsMethod`, the walk stopping at `Player`), which Forge's default doesn't ask for a block needing no tool. On NeoForge a private helper then posts `PlayerEvent.HarvestCheck` with the call's answer, as `doPlayerHarvestCheck` does, so its listeners still decide; in `getDestroyProgress`, where nothing else is asked, vanilla's call and the helper take the hook's place outright. Forge's call fires Forge's event itself. Pre-flight notes each hook ("@WrapOperation apoli$modifyHarvest hooks ServerPlayer.hasCorrectToolForDrops(Lnet/minecraft/world/level/block/state/BlockState;)Z in destroyBlock, vanilla's call NeoForge's ServerPlayerGameMode makes through the block's IBlockExtension.canHarvestBlock instead; Fabricated puts it back there for the blocks keeping that method's default, NeoForge's harvest check event following it as that default posts it, so the hook applies to their harvest check, and a block overriding it is checked as NeoForge has it, without the hook (SEAM-4)"). A native mod's mixin of the moved calls, of the `canHarvestBlock` defaults, of `EventHooks.doPlayerHarvestCheck`, or on Forge of `ForgeHooks.isCorrectToolForDrops` or `Player.hasCorrectToolForDrops`, keeps the class as the platform has it, as SEAM-3's do, the report naming the mixin. NeoForge's event bus is a library outside the game files pre-flight reads: the helper's post of the event counts as there where `doPlayerHarvestCheck` names it the same way (`copied`); without that, pre-flight's view would keep every class it rewrites as it was, SEAM-3's put-back in `ServerPlayerGameMode` with it. A hook of the held item's check in `destroyBlock` stays out, its hint saying why ("NeoForge's ServerPlayerGameMode asks the held item's canAttackBlock inside its block break event (CommonHooks.fireBlockBreak), which can overrule the answer, so Fabricated doesn't put that check back there (SEAM-4)."), with a known-limit line; one in `DoorBlock` is left out with pre-flight's usual reason (no mod in the corpora hooks it). Cost: each check of a block keeping the default takes 47 ns (NeoForge) to 49 ns (Forge) once its classes are known, Forge's check of the player's class 29 ns, the first of each class a reflective lookup (0.2 to 0.6 ms); NeoForge's `getDestroyProgress` asks nothing more. Pre-flight's view of a seam class now expands only the methods it rewrites, and its checks of the members the rewrites name share one index for the view: on NeoForge 26.1.2 the server game mode and `BlockBehaviour` together allocate 5.1 MB as first read, where `main`'s game mode alone took 4.0 MB (the client's game mode 2.8 MB, from 3.9). `VanillaCallsTest` (the fixture's `destroyBlock` and `getDestroyProgress` make each platform's harvest check, with NeoForge's event bus and Forge's event, a block overriding `canHarvestBlock`, one needing no tool and a player class overriding the check: the rewritten classes do what the platform's did for every block, player and tool, a hook answering otherwise runs only on vanilla's branch, the event following it on NeoForge and inside it on Forge; the answers kept by extension; classes put back in parallel, both shapes and both platforms, the same; a game without the event bus, with and without `EventHooks`), `RestoredSeamsTest` (Apoli's shapes on both platforms; the held item's check's hint and limit), `SeamConflictsTest`, `KnownLimitsTest`; the test mod's `vanilla-harvest` check (NeoForge, every run; Forge, with Fabric API; skipped, naming the mixin, where a native mod's mixin keeps the class, which only Forgified Fabric API's runs take as a skip). Verified: core's tests (646) and CI's script tests (62); the inventory on the three branches; NeoForge 1.21.1, 1.21.11 and 26.1.2 and Forge 47, 52 and 66 build. Local servers, every check passing: NeoForge 1.21.1 (server 84, with Fabric API 92, stand-ins 90, with Forgified Fabric API 77) and 26.1.2 (86, 91, 92, 77), Forge 47 (111, 124, 119, 107), 52 (108, 124, 114) and 66 (99, 114, 106); `vanilla-harvest` passes in each and is skipped where Forgified Fabric API shares the game, naming its `ServerPlayerGameMode` mixin. The advanced corpus servers against runs before the change: on NeoForge 1.21.1 and Forge 52 Apoli 2.12's `$HarvestabilityProxy` and `$BlockBreakingDeltaProxy` and its break action apply (mixins left out in whole or in part, 7 to 4 and 5 to 2), the reports' other changes being the test mod's notes, VANILLA-5's and the slow entrypoints' timings; on Forge 47 the test mod's notes and VANILLA-5's; the checks as before, and Forge 47's informational interaction events check, which the earlier run's lagging server stopped before, passing. Porting Lib's hooks were read only: Create isn't in the corpora run here. Discovery on NeoForge 26.1.2's server with Fabric API (the bench's run) allocates 379, 381 and 381 MB, from 376, 377 and 378 on `main`, the limit being 381.7 (the baseline's 347 and 10%); with `main`'s test mod the branch's loader allocates 377, 378 and 379, the rest being what the test mod's two new hooks cost pre-flight and its view. Review fixes: a NeoForge or Forge mod's mixin selecting methods by a name with a quantifier (`tick*`, `<init>*`), with an owner before the name or by a `@Desc` was taken for one selecting any method, so on Forge one of `Player`'s other methods kept the server's game mode and `BlockBehaviour` as Forge has them, SEAM-3's removal with them; selectors are now read as Mixin reads them, a quantifier alone or a regex still selecting any (`SeamConflictsTest.selectorsAreReadAsMixinReadsThem`). A state class overriding `canHarvestBlock` is among the unit tests' blocks. |
| VANILLA-5 | P1 on Forge 52 with Origins 1.13 once SEAM-4 let Apoli's block-break action apply (it would run where a restricted player's break is refused and after a creative break, not after a survival one); on every platform for a hook picking a return by ordinal in a method whose patch adds returns | `CallRepairs.vanillaPointRepairs` (VANILLA-1's repairs), `PreflightAsm.returns` (moved from `ReplacedMethods`) | Forge's server `destroyBlock` returns `false` where the held item's `onBlockStartBreak` says so, after the game master block's return and before vanilla's others: six returns where vanilla's has five (Forge 47 and 52; the break event's return takes the place of vanilla's `canAttackBlock` one). Apoli 2.12's (MIT) `ServerPlayerInteractionManagerMixin.apoli$actionOnBlockBreak` injects at `@At("RETURN", ordinal = 3)` and `ordinal = 4`, vanilla's creative and survival returns; in Forge 52's method those are the restricted player's return and the creative one. Apoli 2.9.2's (MIT; Origins 1.10.2 for 1.20.1) injects at `ordinal = 4` with its locals captured, Forge 47's creative return. | VANILLA-1 read only `INVOKE`, `FIELD` and `NEW` points against vanilla's code; the hook had been left out on Forge (SEAM-4), and NeoForge's `destroyBlock` returns where vanilla's does. | This change: an `@At("RETURN")` with an ordinal, in a method whose vanilla version has fewer returns than this game build's, is pointed at the return VANILLA-1's alignment of the two methods' instructions places vanilla's at, where none of vanilla's returns is gone or shares a return with another (a hook at a return the patch merges with another would run on both paths); otherwise the point stays. The report says so ("@Inject apoli$actionOnBlockBreak expects the return with ordinal 3 in vanilla's code, which has ordinal 4 in this game build, as Forge's patches add returns before it; the injection point is pointed there"). BetterNether's (MIT, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 for some assets; 21.0.11) `MushroomMixin.canStay`, at vanilla's second return of `MushroomBlock.canSurvive`, which NeoForge 21.1 and Forge 52 return once, stays as it was, with pre-flight's warning that the point doesn't occur. Where the patch adds a return made of the same instructions as one of vanilla's next to it, the alignment can pair vanilla's with the patch's: on Forge 52 vanilla's restricted return is paired with Forge's `onBlockStartBreak` one, the same ordinal, so it stays. On Forge 52's and vanilla 1.21.1's `destroyBlock` the alignment places vanilla's returns 3 and 4 at Forge's 4 and 5. `MixinPreflightTest.returnsPickedByOrdinalAreReadAgainstVanillasCode` (a patch adding a return before vanilla's last two; one keeping vanilla's returns; one merging vanilla's creative return into the last, with and without returns added; no vanilla code). Verified: core's tests (646); the advanced corpus servers of Forge 52 and Forge 47 against runs before the change: the report points Apoli 2.12's break action at Forge 52's returns 4 and 5, and Apoli 2.9.2's at Forge 47's return 5, the survival one, where Mixin still skips it for its captured locals (Forge's `destroyBlock` declares an `int` there and not vanilla's removal flag), as it did at the creative return; BetterNether's mushroom hook stays as it was on NeoForge 1.21.1 and Forge 52 (counting returns alone, it would have been pointed at the one return of `canSurvive`, on both paths); no other server's report changes so. Review fixes: an explicit `ordinal = -1`, every return, threw as pre-flight read it in a method whose patch adds returns, so the mod failed to translate; it is left as it is. A return of vanilla's whose place the two alignments disagree on now counts as sharing the picked return's place where either of them pairs it there; Forge 52's `destroyBlock` is pointed as before. |
| COND-1 | P1 on NeoForge 21.1 with Moonlight's NeoForge build (3.8.3) and Fabric API: a Fabric mod's recipe, advancement or other JSON data whose `fabric:all_mods_loaded` names a Fabric mod with a dash in its id, or a Fabric API module Fabricated provides, wasn't loaded | `FabricResourceConditions.decideFirst`; `JsonReloadConditionsMixin` and `SimpleJsonResourceReloadListenerAccessor` (new, 1.21.1's in-game mod); the test mod's `ConditionRereadMixin` and `ConditionsAsPreparedMixin` and its `unparsed.json` (new, its NeoForge 1.21.1 half) | Moonlight's NeoForge build has a mixin (`ConditionHackMixin`) at the start of applying every JSON data loader's files, where upstream's hook is, that reads each file's `fabric:load_conditions` again as NeoForge's conditions (its bridge renames `fabric:` to `neoforge:` and parses them with NeoForge's codec; it registers `neoforge:all_mods_loaded`, which asks NeoForge's mod list) and drops a file they fail. NeoForge's mod list has the NeoForge mod Fabricated makes of a Fabric mod under its id with underscores, and none of the Fabric API modules Fabricated provides, so files upstream's conditions keep were dropped: the test mod's own (`fabricated-testmod`) on a local NeoForge 1.21.1 advanced corpus server with Moonlight's NeoForge build in place of its Fabric build (`json loader conditions FAILED: the loader read []`). The conditions its NeoForge codec can't parse (`any_mods_loaded`, `registry_contains`, `tags_populated`, `features_enabled`) it keeps, and those upstream decides as before; `and`, `or` and `not` around `all_mods_loaded` are read the same way. Moonlight's 26.1.2 NeoForge build has no such mixin, and it has no 1.21.11 NeoForge build. | The corpus has Moonlight's Fabric build, whose bridge reads Fabric's conditions as Fabric does; no run had its NeoForge build, and the test mods' conditioned file is checked only with Fabric API (`server-fapi`). | Fabricated decides a JSON data loader's files by their Fabric conditions first, at the start of applying them (priority 500, before mods' hooks there at the default 1000), with upstream's evaluation and the registries upstream gives the recipe and advancement loaders (NeoForge 21.1 builds both with the lookup it hands every server data loader), and gives each file that loads, in its place in the loader's map, a copy without its conditions: upstream's hook, a moment later, finds none and keeps it, and so does a mod reading them again its own way. A file whose conditions upstream's codec (`ResourceCondition.CONDITION_CODEC`) can't parse is left exactly as it is, as upstream, having decided nothing, keeps it, so a later reader that can parse them (Moonlight's bridge reading `fabric:mod_loaded`, `item_exists`, `tag_empty` or `false` as NeoForge's types) may still drop it; and no prepared object is changed, for a loader whose prepared files outlive a reload (both found in review: as first written, the conditions were taken off every file upstream kept, in place). Without fabric-resource-conditions-api-v1, or where another mod provides it, nothing changes. The test mod's NeoForge 1.21.1 half reads the conditions again as Moonlight does while the module is Fabric API's own, so the `json-loader-conditions` check fails without the fix; and checks, as the loader applies the files, that one naming a condition type no mod registers keeps its conditions and the kept file as prepared keeps its own (`json-loader-conditions-as-they-are`). Verified: CI's script tests and the inventory; NeoForge 1.21.1 builds. A local NeoForge 1.21.1 `server-fapi` run passes every check (89), `json-loader-conditions` among them; with the in-game mixin taken out of its config, the test mod's reading again drops its kept file and the check fails (`the loader read []`), as with Moonlight. A local NeoForge 1.21.1 advanced corpus server with Moonlight's NeoForge build in place of its Fabric build loads the test mod's kept file (`json loader conditions OK`, which failed there before), its other failures as before: CORPUS-113's `/reload` check, and the `vanilla-piston` check, whose hooks find nothing where Moonlight's mixin keeps the resolver (SEAM-1). Review fixes: a local `server-fapi` run passes every check (90), both conditions checks among them, on the first load and after `/reload`, upstream logging once that it can't parse the unparsed file's conditions; with the evaluator as first written, `json-loader-conditions-as-they-are` fails for both reasons. A local NeoForge 1.21.1 advanced corpus server with Moonlight's NeoForge build in place of its Fabric build passes both, keeps the resolver as NeoForge has it, Moonlight's mixin named, and fails only as before (CORPUS-113's `/reload` check and `vanilla-piston`). |
| CI-14 | P3 (CI only) | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, the parts' Mesa EGL install | Every client and mods part of two pull requests' runs failed before any test ran: each attempt's `apt-get update` timed out, as `archive.ubuntu.com` answered 503 while `azure.archive.ubuntu.com`, the image's mirror for the packages, answered; a re-run failed the same way. `apt-get update` reads every source the image lists, and one of them down stops it. | CI-13 bounded an attempt but still refreshed every source first. | This change: each attempt installs Mesa's EGL packages from the image's package lists first, and refreshes them only where that fails. |
| SEAM-3 | P1 on NeoForge 21.1 to 26.3 and Forge 47 to 66 for Wilder Wild's, FrozenLib's and Apoli's block-breaking hooks (left out) and Carpet's (warned about); for any Fabric mod wrapping vanilla's removal in `destroyBlock` | `VanillaCalls` and `KeepsDefault` (new), `VanillaSeams` (the table's second shape, `Restored`, `Rewrite`, `over`), `SeamConflicts`, `MixinPreflight.noteRestoredSeams`, `KnownLimits`; NeoForge's `ServerPlayerGameModeMixin` (1.21.1, common12111) and Forge's `ServerPlayerGameModeBreakMixin` (mc/forge-shared 1.20.1-1.21.1, forge's mc/forge/common); the test mod's `BlockRemovalMixin`, `VanillaBreakTest` and the platforms' `BreakOverride` | Vanilla's `destroyBlock` removes the block with `level.removeBlock(pos, false)`, then `block.destroy` where it removed it (the server's `ServerPlayerGameMode`), or `level.setBlock(pos, fluid.createLegacyBlock(), 11)` (the client's `MultiPlayerGameMode`). NeoForge 21.1.265, 21.11.45 and 26.1.2.116 and Forge 47.4.28, 52.1.21, 61.2.4, 64.1.5, 65.1.5 and 66.1.0 call the block state's `onDestroyedByPlayer` there instead (read in their classes, Forge 47's under SRG's names; NeoForge 26.2.0.90 and 26.3.0.95 in their patches): the server through a private `removeBlock` of theirs, NeoForge's `(pos, state, canHarvest[, tool])` after `canHarvestBlock` and `mineBlock` in survival, which then calls `destroy`, Forge's `(pos, canHarvest)`, which reads the state again. NeoForge's default (`IBlockExtension`) makes vanilla's call (`removeBlock` on the server, `setBlock` on the client); Forge's (`IForgeBlock`) calls `playerWillDestroy`, which Forge's `destroyBlock` no longer makes (NeoForge's keeps it), and `setBlock(pos, fluid, isClientSide ? 11 : 3)`. NeoForge 21.11 on take the tool too. So pre-flight left out, on every version: Wilder Wild's (LGPL-3.0 for 3.0.7, 1.20.1; FrozenBlock Modding Oasis License 1.0 for 4.2.1, 4.2.4 and 4.2.9) `@WrapOperation`s of the server's `ServerLevel.removeBlock` (echo glass and snowlogging, `@Local BlockState`, by ordinal 1 from 1.21.1, by name `adjustedState` on 26.1) and of the client's `Level.setBlock`, with a `@ModifyExpressionValue` of the client's `playerWillDestroy` sharing its state (1.21.1 on); FrozenLib's (GPL-3.0; 3.0.3 for 26.2 and 26.3, none in 1.9.3 to 2.4.4) same server and client hooks (waterlike blocks) and, in its event config, an `@Inject` at the client's `playerWillDestroy` and a `@WrapOperation` of its `Block.destroy`; Apoli's (MIT; Origins 1.13.0-pre.4 for 1.21.1) `@ModifyExpressionValue apoli$cacheBlockRemovedResult` of the server's `removeBlock`, with its break action reading the result through `@Share`; Porting Lib's (LGPL; 2.3.12 to 2.3.15 for 1.20.1, in Create) hooks here are `@Inject`s (the client's `setBlock`, capturing locals) and its `@WrapOperation` of `hasCorrectToolForDrops`, the harvest family; Carpet's (MIT; 1.4.147 on) `@Inject` at the server's `removeBlock`, 1.4.112's at `playerWillDestroy`; Magna's (MIT; 1.10.1, in Modern Industrialization 1.8.6 for Forge 47) `@Inject` at `playerWillDestroy`, which CORPUS-72 moves; FancyMenu's (DSMSL 3.1) `@WrapOperation` of the client's `Block.destroy`, which applies, as NeoForge's and Forge's clients keep that call. In the same methods vanilla asks `player.hasCorrectToolForDrops(state)` where both platforms ask `state.canHarvestBlock(level, pos, player)` (Porting Lib's and Apoli's harvest wrappers, left out: a later family), and Forge drops vanilla's `canAttackBlock` (Porting Lib's `@ModifyExpressionValue`). What the hooks do (Wilder Wild's snow left where a snowlogged block breaks, its echo glass cracking, FrozenLib's waterlike blocks leaving water) didn't happen. | No test hooked a vanilla call the platforms make through an extension. | This change: `VanillaSeams`' table gets a second shape, a row per platform's form of each `destroyBlock`: the platform's call there, the extension it calls and vanilla's calls in its place. Where the class has the call (and the platform's package), each place of it gets, in front of its operands, `fabricated$keepsDefault(state)`: whether neither the state's class nor the block's declares `onDestroyedByPlayer` over the extension's interfaces (`KeepsDefault`: each class, its superclasses and the interfaces they implement extending the extension's, asked by `getDeclaredMethod`, copied into the class as private helpers naming only the JDK; false where it can't tell). Then vanilla's calls are made as their own steps (the server's `this.level.removeBlock(pos, false)` and, where it removed the block, `destroy(level, pos, state)`; the client's `setBlock(pos, fluid.createLegacyBlock(), 11)`, after `block.playerWillDestroy(level, pos, state, player)` on Forge, whose default makes it); otherwise the platform's call, with its operands as before. What vanilla doesn't call there (`getBlock`, Forge's server reading the state and its `playerWillDestroy`, which vanilla makes before the creative check and which CORPUS-72 moves Magna's `@Inject` at to there) goes through private helpers, and the state Forge's server reads is kept in a local of type `Object`, so the platform's calls there keep their counts and no implicit `@Local` sees a new local; vanilla's branch does bring its own calls, field reads (`this.level`) and constants (`false`, `11`), which a native mod's mixin naming them or hooking a constant there keeps the class for (below); operands vanilla's branch needs again are only loaded again, never evaluated twice. The method gets the frames its branches need, worked out on an expanded copy and read back compressed. As SEAM-1: only classes in which a Fabric mixin's injector of the method names a put-back call are rewritten, all or nothing, the class loading as the platform has it where the rewrite throws; pre-flight reads the game through the same rewrite and notes each hook ("@WrapOperation wilderWild$destroyBlockB hooks ServerLevel.removeBlock(Lnet/minecraft/core/BlockPos;Z)Z in destroyBlock, vanilla's call NeoForge's ServerPlayerGameMode makes through the block's IBlockExtension.onDestroyedByPlayer instead; Fabricated puts it back there for the blocks keeping that method's default, so the hook applies to their breaking, and a block overriding it breaks as NeoForge has it, without the hook (SEAM-3)"); a native mod's mixin of the platform's `removeBlock` or `onDestroyedByPlayer` call, of the extensions' defaults, naming a call or field the rewrite adds to `destroyBlock`, hooking a constant there, or, on Forge's server, of `Level.removeBlock` (which vanilla's call put back runs, where Forge's default sets the fluid's block itself, the fluid read before `playerWillDestroy` where vanilla's reads it after), keeps the class as the platform has it (`SeamConflicts`, the decision in the cache key); and pre-flight puts nothing back that names a member the game lacks. NeoForge's and Forge's own `AFTER` break events, which observed the platform's `removeBlock`, also fire at vanilla's `destroy` put back, the block read before vanilla's `removeBlock` (two `@Inject`s, which a mod's `@Redirect` of the calls doesn't take away). On Forge a wrapper of the server's `playerWillDestroy` call stays left out, with a known-limit line. As first written the check asked `Class.getMethod`, which loads the types of every public method of the classes it searches: Forge 66's `IForgeBlock` and `IForgeBlockState` name client classes, absent on a dedicated server, so every block took Forge's path (the test mod's hook "ran 0 times for 2 breaks" on a local Forge 66 server). `VanillaCallsTest` (each platform's form, Forge 47's under SRG's names, server and client, against stand-ins whose extensions name a class the game lacks: before and after, every step, for a block keeping the default, one overriding it, one calling the default from its override and one overriding it through an interface of its own; a hook of the call put back runs for the first alone), `SeamConflictsTest`, `RestoredSeamsTest`, `KnownLimitsTest`; the test mod's `vanilla-break` check (NeoForge, every run; Forge with Fabric API, whose fake player it uses; skipped, naming the mixin, where a native mod's mixin keeps the class as the platform has it, as the report says and the class shows, which only Forgified Fabric API's runs take as a skip): a fake player breaks calcite, which the test mod's `@WrapOperation` of `removeBlock` leaves as tuff, stone, which it removes, and a block of the platform's kind overriding `onDestroyedByPlayer`, which breaks as its override says without the hook. Verified: core's tests (632), CI's script tests and the inventory; the rewrite on each platform's own classes (NeoForge 1.21.1, 1.21.11 and 26.1.2, Forge 52, 61, 64, 65 and 66; Forge 47's at run time) finds every place, and ASM's analyzer passes every method; NeoForge 1.21.1, 1.21.11 and 26.1.2 and Forge 47, 52 and 66 build; local servers pass every check (NeoForge 1.21.1 server and server-fapi: 173; 26.1.2 server: 85; Forge 47: 234; 52: 231; 66: 212), the interaction and block break events checks among them. On the advanced corpus servers, against runs before the change, NeoForge 1.21.1's report has Wilder Wild's, Carpet's and Apoli's removal hooks applying where they were left out or warned about, Apoli's break action still left out (SHARE-2), and Forge 47's Wilder Wild's two; nothing else differs, and the checks are as before (NeoForge 1.21.1: 83 passing with the new one, CORPUS-113's `/reload` failing; Forge 47: 111). Not run: clients (the client rows are checked by the unit tests and ASM only), NeoForge 26.2 and 26.3 and Forge 61, 64 and 65 in game. Review fixes: the helpers' tree, shared between threads, was copied with ASM's `MethodNode.accept`, which resets and sets its labels' state: classes put back in parallel, as translation reads them, swapped labels, threw or failed to verify (NeoForge 1.21.1's class, 8 threads, 3,200 copies: 107 differing, 30 throwing, some with "fabricated$overrides: Execution can fall off the end of the code"), and pre-flight's view let the error through, the class read as absent; each class now reads the helpers afresh, a copy still naming `KeepsDefault` fails, and `VanillaSeams.over` reads a class whose rewrite throws as the platform has it (3,200 copies the same). Each break of a block keeping the default asked `getDeclaredMethod` of every class of the state's and the block's hierarchies, each miss a `NoSuchMethodException` (21,305 ns a break on NeoForge 1.21.1, 21,977 on Forge 66, 60 frames deep), again for a block whose classes name a missing class: the class keeps the answers by class in a private static field (weak keys), made on its first use so its initialiser is as it was (48 and 49 ns), a class that can't be read taken to override, once. Vanilla's one removal, put back in the creative and the survival branch, was picked in the creative one alone by a hook with ordinal 0: the ordinal is dropped where every call there is one put back and vanilla's code makes one (an INFO line; not where the injector allows fewer injections). Pre-flight recorded the class for rewriting, and noted the hook applying, before the config's groupings could leave the hook out (Carpet's piston hook, left out by SEAM-2, was also noted as applying): the notes are the hooks' handlers', dropped with them, and the class recorded for the others. The known limit matched the client's `destroyBlock` too, which Forge 52 on names so: pre-flight's hint for a hook of the call now names the server's class ("Forge's ServerPlayerGameMode makes that call through the block's IForgeBlock.onDestroyedByPlayer instead, which Fabricated doesn't put back there (SEAM-3)."), which the limit matches. A wrapper of vanilla's removal in place of Fabricated's own `AFTER` `@Inject`s loses the event where an outer wrapper doesn't make the call (the test mod's calcite on NeoForge 1.21.1: "PlayerBlockBreakEvents.AFTER fired [-94, 250, -80 minecraft:stone, -92, 250, -80 fabricated_testmod:break_override], not [-96, 250, -80 minecraft:calcite, ...]"), where the `@Inject`s fire it as upstream does; with a `@Redirect` of the removal added to the test mod they still fire it once for each break. Tests: `VanillaCallsTest` (classes put back in parallel, the answers kept by class, a block inheriting an override, a state overriding the state's extension), `SeamConflictsTest` (Forge's `Level.removeBlock`, a constant), `RestoredSeamsTest` (the ordinal, a hook left out by SHARE-1, Forge's hint), `KnownLimitsTest`; `vanilla-break` also has `PlayerBlockBreakEvents.AFTER` fire once for each break with the block broken, on both paths (Forge 47's SRG-named hooks among them). Verified (review fixes): core's tests (637), CI's script tests and the inventory; NeoForge 1.21.1, 1.21.11 and 26.1.2 and Forge 47, 52 and 66 build; local servers pass every check (NeoForge 1.21.1 server and server-fapi: 174; 26.1.2: 85; Forge 47: 234; 52: 231; 66: 212); the advanced corpus servers as before (NeoForge 1.21.1: 83; Forge 47: 111), Apoli's removal hook applying, its break action left out. Then: a mixin left out whole besides pre-flight's own leaving out still had its class recorded for rewriting, its note kept, and the readers of a namespaced local it sets applying with nothing set (a null where they read it): one using a class of an unsupported Fabric API module (left out by translation after the check), one using an interface only a mixin left out adds (the same), and one Mixin rejects whole as it applies (a problem of the whole mixin, a wrapper's arguments not the call's among them, in a config that isn't required: a warning). The first now goes before the check, which takes it as skipped; one Mixin rejects is taken as left out; and translation tells pre-flight of the mixins it leaves out after the check (`MixinPreflight.leftOutAfterCheck`), which records nothing for their hooks and names the readers of their shares to leave out in turn, until no more go. The ordinal repair takes `@At("INVOKE_ASSIGN")` as it takes `INVOKE`. Pre-flight's view read a class whose rewrite throws as the platform has it without a word, its Fabric mods' hooks left out as if the platform's code had changed: the report's environment now names the class and what it threw ("Vanilla's methods not put back in PistonStructureResolver: putting vanilla's method back failed as pre-flight read the class (...), so it is read, and loads, as NeoForge has it; ... (SEAM-1)"). Tests: `RestoredSeamsTest` (a mixin skipped, one Mixin rejects, one left out after the check; `INVOKE_ASSIGN`), `PipelineTest` (a mixin using the renderer API's class and one using its interface, with readers of what each shares; a rewrite failing). Verified: core's tests (640) and CI's script tests on the three branches, and their inventories; the six builds; local servers pass every check (NeoForge 1.21.1 server and server-fapi: 174; Forge 47 server-fapi: 123); the NeoForge 1.21.1 advanced corpus server's report and checks are those of the run before (83 passing). |
| SHARE-2 | P2 on NeoForge 21.1 with Origins once SEAM-3 let Apoli's removal hook apply; on every platform for a hook reading what another mixin's left-out hook shares with it | `MixinPreflight.leaveOutNamespacedSharers` (new) | MixinExtras' `@Share` with a namespace shares a local between every mixin's injectors into the method. Apoli's (MIT; 2.12.0-pre.4 in Origins 1.13.0-pre.4) `ServerPlayerInteractionManagerMixin` break action reads the broken block from `breakingBlock` (namespace `apoli`), which its `ModifyHarvestPowerTypeMixin$HarvestabilityProxy` keeps beside its harvest wrapper, left out where the platforms ask their own harvest check; with SEAM-3 the break action's other share (`blockRemoved`) applied, so it would have read no block for a player with an `action_on_block_break` power (read in Apoli's classes and the advanced corpus's report). | SHARE-1 followed a mod's shares within one mixin, and no test had two mixins sharing a local. | This change: once a config's mixins are checked, a left-out injector takes with it those of its other mixins sharing a namespaced local with it, and in turn their own mixins' sharers, with the reason ("The injector of apoli$actionOnBlockBreak(...) goes with that of ...HarvestabilityProxy.apoli$modifyHarvest(...), which can't apply: they share the local breakingBlock (namespace apoli) (@Share), ..."). Apoli's break action and its removal hook stay out until the harvest family. `MixinPreflightTest.anInjectorSharingANamespacedLocalWithOneOfAnotherMixinLeftOutGoesWithIt`. Verified with SEAM-3: core's tests, and the NeoForge 1.21.1 advanced corpus server's report and checks. Review fixes: it went both ways, in any method of the config's: an injector only reading a local took the one setting it, and two injecting into different methods, whose locals MixinExtras keeps apart, went together; and it started only from the config's left-out injectors. Now a left-out injector takes only those that may read a local it may set (by its code, the reference followed on the stack and through the locals: a `get` or a `set` with it as their receiver; anything else, a lambda or a method taking it, or a set of another reference to it, may do both), that inject into a method in common, from mixins skipped too (the user's, the plan's, those left out at the last launch, those using an unsupported module's class), from mixins Mixin rejects whole and those translation leaves out after the check, and across the mod's configs, one checked later leaving out one checked before, from its findings. Apoli's removal hook (`cacheBlockRemovedResult`) now applies, as its break action only reads what it sets; the break action stays out, reading `modifiedHarvest`, which only the harvest hook sets. With SEAM-4 the harvest hook applies, and the break action with it (on Forge 52 at vanilla's returns, VANILLA-5). `MixinPreflightTest.namespacedSharersGoWithAnInjectorSettingTheLocalInTheirMethodAcrossTheModsConfigs` (with a reference handed on before another's set, and one set as another's value). |
| SEAM-2 | P1 on NeoForge 21.1, 21.11 and 26.1.2 and Forge 47 with Carpet's `chainStone` rule on, once SEAM-1 let Carpet's redirect apply (a `NullPointerException` as a piston pushes a chain along its axis); for any mod whose hooks read the state a locals capture keeps where Mixin skips the capture | `SkippedCaptures` (new), `MixinPreflight.checkInjector`, `MixinPreflight.Context.skippedCapture`; `LocalRepairs.softenLocalCapture` (the soften step, LOCAL-5), STATE-1's state groups; Carpet's (MIT) `PistonStructureResolver_customStickyMixin` | Carpet's `@Redirect onAddBlockLineCanStickToEachOther` of `addBlockLine`'s `canStickToEachOther` call hands its chain and chest code the positions its `@Inject captureBlockLinePositions` (`CAPTURE_FAILHARD`, at the method's second `getBlockState`) keeps in the mixin's fields `pos_addBlockLine` and `behindPos_addBlockLine`. That `@Inject` captures `(BlockState, int, BlockPos)`; NeoForge 21.1.265, 21.11.45 and 26.1.2.116 and Forge 47.4.28 declare `addBlockLine`'s old state in slot 5 and vanilla's position in slot 6, storing the position first (read in their classes, Forge 47's at runtime), so Mixin finds `(BlockState, int, BlockState)` there ("LVT ... has incompatible changes ... Expected: [BlockState, I, BlockPos] Found: [BlockState, I, BlockState]") and, the soften step having made the capture skip itself, skips it: the fields stay null. With `chainStone` on (default off), a piston placed to push a chain along its axis asks Carpet's `ChainBlock.isStickyToNeighbor` with a null position as it resolves the push, which reaches `Block.canSupportCenter`: a `NullPointerException` in `PistonBaseBlock.checkIfExtend` (a local NeoForge 1.21.1 advanced corpus server with SEAM-1, the rule set in the world's `carpet.conf` and the piston placed by a scarpet app). Forge 52 and 61 to 66 keep vanilla's order, and the capture applies there. | The soften step made such captures skip themselves instead of stopping the game, and nothing judged what read the state they keep; SEAM-1's runs showed Mixin's warning, but nothing pushed a chain with the rule on. | This change: pre-flight tells where Mixin skips a locals capture at every point (`SkippedCaptures`). Mixin compares the handler's captured parameters with as many of the locals it finds past the target's parameters, slots holding none skipped, exactly but for a `@Coerce` (`CallbackInjector.Callback.checkDescriptor`, `Target.getCallbackDescriptor`, the same in SpongePowered's 0.8.5 and Fabric's 0.16 and 0.17); it finds them walking the method up to the point (`Locals.getLocalsAt`, and Fabric's `getLocalsAt092`), a store giving its slot the local variable table's entry covering the next instruction, a load the one covering the load, any frame possibly another. So a slot's local is known where the last thing to give it one is a store or load after the last frame before the point, an entry covering it; only one unshifted `@At` at a call, field access or the method's returns is judged, without a slice, in targets with a table, the handler without `@Coerce` or a method of its name besides it. Such a capture is noted (INFO), not left out, so no grouping takes anything with it; after STATE-1, SHARE-1 and ITEM-2 have run, the injectors left reading what only skipped captures store in the mixin's own fields (`SkippedCaptures.unsupplied`: a `PUTFIELD` or `PUTSTATIC`, itself or through the mixin's methods, by no injector that still applies, the reader itself included, nor by the mixin's initialisers), and in turn what only those store, are left out alone, and only where STATE-1 could leave them out alone (their group of state in `stateGroups`: no code but the mixin's injectors reaches it, the mixin self-contained) and none shares a `@Share` local or a mod's `ThreadLocal` with an injector that stays; otherwise nothing changes and the report says why they stay. Never the whole mixin, never a FAIL: no outcome worse than Mixin's skipping the capture, but for reads of state never stored. An alternative capture in the platform's order storing the same field keeps everything. On these platforms Carpet's line redirect is left out, as before SEAM-1 ("@Inject captureBlockLinePositions captures the locals (BlockState, int, BlockPos) in addBlockLine(BlockPos, Direction), where Mixin finds (BlockState, int, BlockState) in NeoForge's code at each of its points: Mixin skips it there (SEAM-2)", "The injector of onAddBlockLineCanStickToEachOther(...) goes with that of captureBlockLinePositions(...), which Mixin skips at each of its points or which can't apply: it reads what only captureBlockLinePositions(...) stores, in this mixin's fields behindPos_addBlockLine, pos_addBlockLine (SEAM-2)"); its `isSticky` hook and its redirect of `addBranchingBlocks`, whose capture applies, stay. `@At("NEW")` points aren't judged: Mixin 0.8.7, 0.16 and 0.17 inject at the `NEW` and count the ordinal over every `NEW` of the class, 0.8.5 otherwise. `SkippedCapturesTest` (Carpet's shape compiled with each order, alternative captures in each order, the position kept in a public field, read by a non-private method or an `@Overwrite`, a mixin adding an interface, a frame between the stores and the point, the softened form, a `NEW` point). As first written this change left the capture out, so STATE-1 took every injector sharing a field with it, alternatives that applied among them, or the whole mixin where the state reached other code (found in review). Verified: core's tests (595), CI's script tests and the mixin inventory; NeoForge 1.21.1 and 26.1.2 and Forge 47 built with the change. On local advanced corpus servers before and after it (all else the same), the reports differ by those two lines alone on NeoForge 1.21.1 and Forge 47 (Forge 47's naming `m_60433_`), as do the resolver's lines on NeoForge 26.1.2 against SEAM-1's run; Mixin no longer warns of the capture, and every check is as before (NeoForge 1.21.1: 79 passing, CORPUS-113's `/reload` failing; Forge 47: 108; NeoForge 26.1.2: 81). The main corpus servers of NeoForge 1.21.1 and Forge 47 report the same before and after, their checks too (81 and 119 passing). With `chainStone` on, the push that threw before the change moves the chain and the stone after it, on NeoForge 1.21.1 and Forge 47, with no exception. Not run: clients, NeoForge 21.11 (its class has the same order). After the review fixes: core's tests (613), CI's script tests and the inventory; NeoForge 1.21.1 and Forge 47 built; on their advanced corpus servers, against the runs before SEAM-2, the only line newly left out is Carpet's line redirect, the capture an INFO line (Mixin warns of it as it skips it), every check passing but CORPUS-113's on NeoForge 1.21.1 (81 and 110 passing), and with `chainStone` on the push completes without an exception on both. Not done: a repair rewriting such a capture into MixinExtras `@Local`s, each the local vanilla's order has there, would let Carpet's chains and chests stick along the line too; it needs the platform's locals matched to vanilla's (`LocalRepairs.isVanillasLocal`), the locals SpongePowered's 0.8.5 keeps past their scope counted on Forge 47 (PRE-26) and runs of each version, so it is left for a later change. |
| SEAM-1 | P1 on NeoForge 21.1, 21.11 and 26.1.2 and Forge 47 to 66 for Carpet's, FrozenLib's and Moonlight's sticky blocks (their hooks left out); for any Fabric mod hooking vanilla's piston stickiness methods | `VanillaSeams` (new), `MixinPreflight` (its notes), `PreflightClasses.mixinTarget`, `Pipeline`, `ModTranslator`, `TranslatedMod`; `loader/common`'s `FabricClassTransforms`; forge-legacy's `mc/platform-forge-legacy.gradle` (the test mod) | Vanilla's `PistonStructureResolver` asks its private static `isSticky(state)` (once in `resolve`, twice in `addBlockLine`) and `canStickToEachOther(state1, state2)` (in `addBlockLine` and `addBranchingBlocks`). NeoForge 21.1.265, 21.11.45 and 26.1.2.116 and Forge 47.4.28, 52.1.21, 61.2.4, 64.1.5, 65.1.5 and 66.1.0 took both out and ask `state.isStickyBlock()` and `state1.canStickTo(state2) && state2.canStickTo(state1)` there (read in their classes, Forge 47's under SRG's names; NeoForge 26.1.2's `addBlockLine` asks `oldState.canStickTo(nextState) && oldState.canStickTo(oldState)`). NeoForge 26.2.0.90 and 26.3.0.95 keep both methods, their bodies asking the extensions, and their calls (read in their patches). So pre-flight left out, on every other version: Carpet's (MIT; 1.4.112 for 1.20.1, 1.4.147 for 1.21.1, 1.4.194 for 1.21.11, the 26.1, 26.2 and 26.3 builds) `PistonStructureResolver_customStickyMixin`, an `@Inject` at `isSticky`'s start, `@Redirect`s of the `canStickToEachOther` calls in `addBlockLine` and `addBranchingBlocks` and, from 1.21.1, an `@Shadow` of it, beside two `@Inject`s keeping positions from the locals at `getBlockState`'s second calls; FrozenLib's (GPL-3.0; 2.3 for 1.21.11 and 2.4.4 for 26.1, `DoubleChestStickingMixin`; 3.0.3 for 26.2 and 26.3, `PistonStructureResolverMixin`; none in 1.9.3 or 2.2.4) `@WrapOperation`s of the `isSticky` calls in `resolve` and `addBlockLine` and of the `canStickToEachOther` calls in both; Moonlight's (Supplementaries Team License 1.5; 3.8.3 for 1.21.1 and 4.2.2 for 26.1.2, no Fabric build for 1.21.11 or 26.2 on, none in 1.20.1's) same three `@WrapOperation`s. What they make sticky (FrozenLib's double chests, Carpet's chains and chests with its rules on, Moonlight's directional sticky blocks) didn't stick. | No test hooked a vanilla method a platform took out of a class, and pre-flight read the game as the platform has it. | This change: `VanillaSeams` is a table of such methods, each with the platform's code where vanilla calls it, calls of its parameters joined by `&&`. Where a class lacks the method and has that code, the method is declared again with that code as its body (under its SRG name on Forge 47) and those places call it: the platform's first call becomes the method's, its later calls, which only loaded locals, go, so every check answers as the platform's code did and the stack is the same at every instruction. NeoForge 26.1.2's line check is restored as the call too, so with a Fabric mod's hooks it asks both ways, as its other check and NeoForge 26.2 do. Pre-flight reads the game through the same rewrite, notes each hook in the report ("@Inject isSticky hooks isSticky(…)Z, vanilla's method Forge's PistonStructureResolver no longer has; Fabricated puts it back there, answering a.isStickyBlock() as Forge's code did (SEAM-1)") and records the class of each (kept with the translation, schema 42): only where a mixin member names or shadows a seam, so a mixin of the class hooking none (Lithium's of `Direction.values()` in the resolver) leaves it as the platform has it (`MixinPreflight.noteRestoredSeams`; as first written, any mixin targeting the class had it rewritten, found in review). `loader/common`'s before-Mixin transform, every platform's, puts them back in those classes as they load, and in no other, all or nothing (`VanillaSeams.restoreWhole`, on a copy: where it throws, the class loads as the platform has it and one error names it). A rewritten class no longer calls the platform's extensions where vanilla called the seams, so a native mod's mixin of those calls would find none there: Moonlight's NeoForge build (3.8.3 for 1.21.1) wraps the resolver's `isStickyBlock` and `canStickTo` calls in `resolve`, `addBlockLine` and `addBranchingBlocks`, required (`defaultRequire` 1), so with a Fabric mod hooking the seams (Carpet, FrozenLib for 26.x) Mixin would have stopped the game (found in review). Fixed: `SeamConflicts` tells, from the installed native mods' mixins (their annotations, read as pre-flight reads installed jars), whether one hooks what the rewrite would move or shift (a moved call named in any injector's annotations, a wildcard target or selector, a selector naming no method of the class, a descriptor selector, a point a move may shift in a method holding moved calls, an overwrite of one, a mixin that can't be read); such a class is left as the platform has it, in pre-flight's view (`Pipeline`, `VanillaSeams.over`) and so at load, the Fabric mods' hooks of its seams finding nothing as before SEAM-1, each noted ("@WrapOperation moonlight$stickAlongLine hooks canStickToEachOther(...)Z, vanilla's method NeoForge's PistonStructureResolver no longer has, which Fabricated doesn't put back there, so it finds nothing to hook, as before SEAM-1: NeoForge mod Moonlight Lib's mixin PistonStructureResolverMixin hooks the platform's code that putting vanilla's method back would move (SEAM-1)"), and the report's environment names the class and the mixin. The decision is part of the translation cache key (`TranslationEnvironment.seamsKept`), and is itself kept in the cache from one launch to the next (`seams-<hash>.json`), keyed by the native jars' paths and file stamps, the seam classes and the environment, as reading every listed native mixin took about 140 ms for 5,000 of them at each launch: a hit takes about 2 ms, a damaged file or a changed jar is decided again. Only a decision every read of which succeeded is stored (found in review): a list of a jar's mixins (a mods.toml, a manifest, a mixin config), a mixin or a seam class failing to read (EIO on a network mount, a sync client holding the file) keeps the class for that launch, conservatively, and the next launch decides again; a seam class failing to read, an error from the platform's transformers among them, no longer reaches the locator's catch-all, which disabled Fabric support for the launch. `SeamConflictsTest` reads a list, then a mixin, failing on one launch and cleanly on the next, and a seam class failing to read. `SeamConflictsTest`, `PipelineTest.vanillaMethodsArentPutBackWhereANativeModsMixinHooksThePlatformsCode` (the decision through translation, then the methods put back for a native mixin hooking what stays, then a cache hit), `NativeModMixinsTest`. On NeoForge the installed jars didn't list their mods' mixins, which Forge's did: now they do, from the configs a mod's mods.toml declares (`[[mixins]]`) and its manifest names (`NeoForgeMods`, `NativeModMixins.declaredMixins`), so pre-flight also sees the members a NeoForge mod's mixin adds, as on Forge; a mixin whose bytes don't name the class isn't parsed. Verified: core's tests (619), CI's script tests and the inventory; NeoForge 1.21.1 and 26.1.2 build; on a local NeoForge 1.21.1 advanced corpus server with Moonlight's NeoForge build (3.8.3) in place of its Fabric build (and Amendments', which needs that one), beside Carpet's Fabric build, the server starts, the resolver isn't rewritten, the report's environment says "Vanilla's methods not put back in PistonStructureResolver: NeoForge mod Moonlight Lib's mixin PistonStructureResolverMixin hooks the platform's code that putting vanilla's method back would move; Fabric mods' hooks of them find nothing there (SEAM-1)", and each of Carpet's four hooks and the test mod's three has its line, Carpet's mixin left out as before SEAM-1; the test mod's `vanilla-piston` check fails there, its hooks finding nothing, as it should, and its json loader conditions check too (Moonlight's NeoForge build has a mixin of NeoForge's conditions; not followed up), the other checks as without Moonlight's NeoForge build (79 passing, CORPUS-113's failing). The other seams the platforms replaced (block breaking, tool and shears checks) can be rows of the table. `RestoredSeamsTest` (a mixin hooking no seam records nothing; an injector or a shadow of one records the class), `VanillaSeamsTest` (the rewrite at load failing leaves the class as it was; classes shaped as the platforms', each form, a class keeping the methods, SRG names; loaded and run against the original for every pair of states), `MixinPreflightTest.hooksOfVanillasStickinessMethodsApplyWhereTheyArePutBack`, `PipelineTest`; the test mod's `vanilla-piston` check (an `@Inject` at `isSticky`'s start, at a priority that runs it before other mods' hooks there, and `@WrapOperation`s of the calls make smooth basalt sticky but not to calcite; slime still pulls stone and not honey), on every platform and version. Where the platform orders `addBlockLine`'s locals otherwise (NeoForge 21.1 to 26.1.2, Forge 47), Carpet's capture of the line's positions finds other locals, and it is left out with the line redirect reading them (SEAM-2).
