Verified: the rewrite on each platform's own class (NeoForge 1.21.1, 1.21.11 and 26.1.2, Forge 47 at runtime under SRG's names, 52, 61, 64, 65 and 66) finds the five places vanilla's 1.20.1 and 26.1.2 classes call the methods, and ASM's analyzer passes every method; local servers with the test mods pass every check (NeoForge 1.21.1: 74, 26.1.2: 75; Forge 47: 99, 52: 100, 66: 91); on the local advanced corpus servers Carpet's, FrozenLib's and Moonlight's hooks of the resolver are noted as applying and none is left out (until SEAM-2, which leaves Carpet's line capture and redirect out on NeoForge 1.21.1 and 26.1.2 and Forge 47; Carpet's on all five, FrozenLib's on NeoForge 26.1.2 and Forge 66, Moonlight's on NeoForge 1.21.1 and 26.1.2 and Forge 52), the new check passes, and the other checks are as before: NeoForge 26.1.2 and Forge 47 pass all 76 and 99, NeoForge 1.21.1 and Forge 52 fail only CORPUS-113's `/reload` check, Forge 66 only CORPUS-114's two ingredient checks and REG-13's entrypoint check. |
| REG-13 | P1 on Forge 66 for Wover Story (its main entrypoint fails; the advanced corpus runs disable it); on Forge 47, 52 and 61 to 66 for any two equal entries of a registry Forge keeps | Forge's `ForgeRegistry` (its `ids`, `names`, `keys` and `owners` are Guava `HashBiMap`s, which key a value by its `equals`); vanilla's `PoiType`, a record; `KnownLimits` (new, core), `EntrypointRunner.Failure.describe` | Wover registers two point-of-interest types with no block states and the same tickets and range, `PoiManager.register(id, Set.of(), 0, 1, tag)` for `wover:portal` and `wover:story_altar` (read in WorldWeaver 26.302.1's classes): as records they are equal, and `ForgeRegistry.add` takes the second for the first registered again (`IllegalArgumentException`: "The object PoiType[matchingStates=[], maxTickets=0, validRange=1]{36b858fa} has been registered twice, using the names wover:portal and wover:story_altar. (Other object at this id is ...{4bef7d97})"; read in Forge 52.1.21's source and Forge 47.4.28's and 66.1.0's classes). Vanilla's `MappedRegistry` keys values by identity (`IdentityHashMap`, `Reference2IntOpenHashMap`; read in 26.3's classes), so on Fabric and NeoForge they are two entries; it only pauses in an IDE on the same object added twice. A Forge mod registering two equal records fails the same way. Not POI-3's. The entrypoint failure showed only Forge's exception. | No test registers two equal values; the advanced corpus runs, which do, are informational. | Known limit, not fixed: Forge's maps can't hold two equal values (a `BiMap` refuses a value already present), and keying them by identity would change, for every Forge mod, what a lookup by value (`getKey`, `getID`, the delegates) finds for an equal object. The fix is Wover's (types that differ, or their states given as they are registered) or Forge's (identity maps); a workaround in Fabricated would serve this one mod. The failure of two such objects, told apart by the two identity hashes in Forge's message, in the report, the log and Forge's loading warnings, now ends "Known limit: Forge's registries take two equal entries for one, so the mod's second one can't be registered; use NeoForge or the mod's Forge build (REG-13)"; one object registered under two names, the same hash twice, isn't REG-13 and gets no hint (`KnownLimitsTest`). |
| SCREEN-2 | P1 on Forge 65 and 66 clients with Architectury, FancyMenu or SpruceUI (their hooks around drawing a screen don't run: Architectury's screen render events among them); on Forge 65 and 66 for any Fabric mod wrapping the screen's render call | Forge 65's and 66's `ForgeLayerInstance`, which `Gui` extends; `KnownLimits` (new, core), `Pipeline` | Forge 65 and 66 draw the screen in `ForgeLayerInstance.drawScreen(graphics, x, y, delta)`, which `Gui` inherits and calls on itself: it reads the screen itself (`this.screen()`) and calls `extractRenderStateWithTooltipAndSubtitles` through a static helper once for each background layer and once for the screen (read in Forge 66's classes; Forge 65 has the same class, Forge 64 none). Architectury's `MixinGui`, FancyMenu's `MixinFabricGameRenderer` and SpruceUI's `fabric.GuiMixin` wrap that call in `extractRenderState` with `@WrapOperation`s, which pre-flight leaves out (the release's runs, 42805036593: the 26.2 and 26.3 client corpus and advanced clients), and the report said only that the mod needs an update. On Forge 61 and 64 Forge's static `ForgeHooksClient.drawScreen(screen, graphics, x, y, delta)` takes the screen, and HOOK-2 points Architectury's and FancyMenu's wrappers at its call there; SpruceUI's older `GameRendererMixin` is left out on Forge 61 and 64 as on NeoForge 1.21.11, for another reason (UNVERIFIED). | The client test mod wraps no screen render call, and the corpus runs are informational. | Known limit, not fixed: the wrappers take the screen as the call's receiver and pass it on, and a wrapper of Forge's call could neither give them the receiver Forge's method takes nor make the call with the one they pass, so HOOK-2's shape (a static hook taking the receiver) doesn't hold; `@Inject`s there move (HOOK-3). The report's line of a wrapper left out (or disabling its mod) now ends "Known limit: Forge draws screens through its own layer code, so wrappers of a screen's render call can't apply; use NeoForge or the mod's Forge build (SCREEN-2)" (`KnownLimitsTest`). |

Pre-flight left the hook out ("captures the local variable of type LayeredDraw, which <init> in Gui does not declare", the fourth round's 1.21.1 advanced client); the game ran without Apoli's bars. It runs as NeoForge's constructor adds vanilla's first stack to its root manager, which vanilla's read comes right before: a wrapper of the root's `add(GuiLayerManager, BooleanSupplier)` there, which requires nothing, tells NeoForge's stacks by the layers they hold, not by the locals holding them. Where no stack is found so (another 21.1 build, another mod's change), the seam runs as the constructor ends, and the report names the layers hooks added, which aren't drawn then; nothing fails. Pre-flight points an `@Inject` whose every point is a read of a field the constructor it targets never makes at that field's seam, where its target has one, so the hook applies as written, whatever it captures there. The stacks the seam holds are vanilla's, a placeholder at each of vanilla's layers; as NeoForge adds each stack, each layer the hooks added goes into NeoForge's manager of it as a layer of its own (`fabricated:vanilla_stack_layer_<n>`), in the stack's order, before NeoForge's first layer of the vanilla layer after it (vanilla's hotbar layer is NeoForge's ten from `HOTBAR` to `SPECTATOR_TOOLTIP`), or where vanilla's stack ends (before the saving indicator NeoForge ends the second with); NeoForge then copies it into its root under the stack's condition. NeoForge's layers stay as they are, in its order, and its mods' (`RegisterGuiLayersEvent`, after the constructor) order against them as before. A vanilla layer a hook takes out of its list, or replaces, isn't drawn: NeoForge's layers for it draw nothing, under their names, as NeoForge's own `replaceLayer` keeps a layer, and a replacement is drawn where it was; one a hook moves stays where NeoForge draws it. Mixin takes an injector at a field access or a call in a method another mixin merged only from a mixin of higher priority (`Injector.findTargetNodes`): the seam's mixin has the lowest priority, pre-flight reads the seam's code and priority as Mixin merges it (`RestoredFields`) and repairs a hook only from a mixin of higher priority. It makes the seam anew on each read of `Gui` (mods are translated in parallel), and marks every method Fabricated's mixins add as Mixin marks a merged one, so a selector matching any number of methods leaves them out, HUD-4's seams and the bridges too, as Mixin does. Other hooks in the constructor capturing a stack stay left out with the line above. `MixinPreflightTest.anInjectionAtAReadThePlatformsConstructorNeverMakesIsPointedAtFabricatedsSeam` and `anInjectionElsewhereInTheConstructorOrAtAReadThePlatformMakesStaysWhereItIs`, `RestoredFieldsTest.aSeamForAReadKeepsItsCodeAsMixinMergesIt` (reads in parallel among it); NeoForge 1.21.1's test mod adds a layer as Apoli does and replaces vanilla's demo overlay layer, and checks each is drawn once as a layer of its own where vanilla draws it (between NeoForge's spectator tooltip and experience level layers; between its demo and debug overlay layers, its demo overlay layer drawing vanilla's no more), and neither with the GUI hidden (`neoforge-client-hud-vanilla-layer`, a gate). Verified on a local NeoForge 1.21.1 client with the test mods: the report points the test mod's hook at the seam ("@Inject fabricatedTest$afterTheHotbar injects at the read of layers in <init>(Lnet/minecraft/client/Minecraft;)V, which NeoForge's constructor never makes; the injection point is pointed at Fabricated's seam fabricated$read$layers") and the check passes, with every other (42 passing, 3 skipped), the replacement's included; the server passes (80); with Fabric API too (70 passing), Fabric API's HUD callback among them. With the advanced corpus the report points Apoli's hook there ("@Inject apoli$renderResourceBars injects at the read of layers ..."), Mixin mixes Apoli's `InGameHudMixin` into `Gui` without an error ("Mixing InGameHudMixin from apoli.mixins.json into net.minecraft.client.gui.Gui", after Fabricated's seam mixin), the client builds its HUD, the check passes there and the client waits on Origins' `ChooseOriginScreen` until its time limit, as before (CORPUS-66). A NeoForge 26.1.2 client passes, the check skipped there. With the stacks never found (a scratch build), the client runs, the test mod's check fails as it should (its two layers not drawn), and the report's Rendering section names the layer each stack lost. Apoli's own bars in a drawing are UNVERIFIED (no origin is chosen in the run's world). |

Nothing asks the plugin anything: Mixin does, as on Fabric, no earlier and no more often. Asking it during translation isn't safe: it is mod code (Pehkui's reads Fabric Loader's mod list), and nothing runs mod code there. A mixin pre-flight leaves out whole stays listed, so that Mixin asks the plugin about it, and the plugin, as translated, turns it down whatever it answers; Mixin reads such a class and its targets' names before it asks, as on Fabric. Mixin may apply it all the same, so the translated jar has it inert (`ObservedPlugins.inert`): an empty public interface of the same `@Mixin`, marked `@Pseudo`. Mixin's `PluginHandle` (SpongePowered's 0.8.5 and 0.8.7, Fabric's 0.16 and 0.17) answers yes for every mixin of a config whose plugin it couldn't load or construct ("Error loading companion plugin class"), and the package guard may serve another jar's copy of a plugin in a package around the mixin package's; the first version kept the mixin as the mod has it, which then applied and failed, stopping the game in a required config. Of the empty interface Mixin reads the targets' names, checks that none was loaded too early and asks the plugin, as of the mixin's; takes it as an accessor mixin, which may target a class or an interface and whose superclass is `Object`; skips a target that isn't there; and applies nothing but the interface itself. A library's plugin (MixinConstraints') isn't observed: another jar's copy may be the one the package guard serves. The report, as it is written once Mixin has selected the configs, lists the mixins a plugin turned down apart, by config, under "Mixins their configs' plugins leave out (as on Fabric)", and no longer counts them in the mod's row. `ObservedPluginsTest`, `PipelineTest.theMixinsAConfigsPluginTurnsDownAreListedApartFromThoseLeftOut`, `PipelineTest.aMixinLeftOutWholeAppliesNothingWhereItsConfigsPluginFailsToLoad` and `aMixinLeftOutWholeAppliesNothingWhereAnotherJarsCopyOfItsConfigsPluginIsServed`. The test mod's plugin turns down a mixin pre-flight leaves out (`plugin-turned-down`) and would apply another, which the translated plugin turns down (`plugin-asked-left-out`), and the server starts; a broken test mod's plugin fails to load (`fabricated-testmod-badplugin`, `plugin-failed-left-out`), Mixin applies the inert mixin it keeps listed, and the server starts; the first build's fixture, a shadow pre-flight takes for another mixin's field, applied and stopped the server, as it would on Fabric. Forge wrote the report after the entrypoints only when one failed, so a Forge client whose Fabric mods all started kept discovery's report, from before Mixin's selection: `EntrypointOutcome` now writes it then in any case, as NeoForge's in-game mod does as loading completes. Verified on local advanced servers: Pehkui's left-out lines fall from the release run's 188 to 3 on NeoForge 1.21.1 and from 132 to 9 on Forge 47, each left one its plugin applies on that version, the new section listing 113 and 79 of its mixins; on the NeoForge 1.21.1 corpus client ImmediatelyFast's `MixinGL11`, which its plugin turned down there, moves there too, and no other left-out line changed. The test mod's checks pass on local NeoForge 1.21.1, Forge 47 and Forge 66 servers and show on the Forge 47 corpus client. With the inert mixins, on local NeoForge 1.21.1 (Fabric's Mixin 0.16.4) and Forge 47 (SpongePowered's 0.8.5) servers Mixin logs the broken test mod's plugin failing ("Error loading companion plugin class [io.github.cmdralph.fabricated.testmod.broken.plugin.FailingPlugin]"), mixes `LeftOutMixin` into `MinecraftServer`, and the server starts; the Forge 47 advanced server keeps Pehkui's 9 left-out lines and the 79 mixins its plugin leaves out. |
| PRE-29 | P2 on every platform with Lithium (its lazy collision context, left out of every corpus run) | `MixinPreflight.acceptsModifierSignature`, `InjectionPoints.constantOf` | Pre-flight refused a `@ModifyConstant` of a class constant whose handler takes the object and the class, `(Object, Class)Z`, as not matching the target's parameters, and left the whole mixin out: Lithium's `EntityCollisionContextMixin` (`EntityShapeContextMixin` in its 1.20.1 build), whose handlers replace vanilla's `instanceof LivingEntity` checks in `EntityCollisionContext`'s constructor (ordinals 0 and 2), on all 11 platforms. Mixin's `ModifyConstantInjector` hands an `instanceof` it finds for a `classValue` to `RedirectInjector.injectAtInstanceOf`, which takes a handler returning `boolean` or `Class` and taking exactly the object and the class (not coercible), then the target's first parameters (`validateParams`), and refuses any other (read in Fabric's Mixin 0.17.4 and SpongePowered's 0.8.5). Pre-flight also counted only `ldc` class constants, where Mixin's `Bytecode.getConstant` counts a cast and an `instanceof` of the class too. | No test had an `instanceof` constant; the first version's none where Mixin finds a cast or a class literal. | This change: such a handler is accepted in those two shapes only, the target's first parameters after them, and refused with another return type; a cast or an `instanceof` is its class's constant, so ordinals count as Mixin counts them. Each instruction Mixin picks for a class constant (the ordinal-th, or each without an ordinal; none within a slice) is checked against the handler: an `instanceof` takes the object and the class, a class literal (`ldc`) the class, and Mixin refuses a cast (`ModifyConstantInjector`: "does not support CHECKCAST insn"); a mismatch leaves the mixin out, naming the constant's ordinal and why (`MixinPreflight.refusedClassConstant`). The first version took an `(Object, Class)` handler wherever a `@Constant` has a `classValue`, so where the ordinal-th class constant is a cast or a class literal it passed, and Mixin threw as the class loaded. `MixinPreflightTest.anInstanceofConstantsHandlerTakesTheObjectAndTheClass`. The test mod modifies the `instanceof` in `Vec3i.equals`, the cast after it being the second constant (`instanceof-constant`). Verified on local corpus clients: Lithium's mixin applies on NeoForge 1.21.1 and Forge 47, its two lines gone, and no other left-out line changed but those of LAMBDA-11, LAMBDA-12 and LEAVE-4; the test mod's check passes on local NeoForge 1.21.1, Forge 47 and Forge 66 servers. With the instructions checked, the test mod's check passes on local NeoForge 1.21.1 and Forge 47 servers, and Lithium's two hooks (`redirectInstanceOf` and `redirectInstanceOf2`, the `instanceof`s at ordinals 0 and 2) apply on the Forge 47 corpus server. |
| LAMBDA-12 | P1 on Forge 47 clients with Architectury 9.2 (its screen click and release events) | `LambdaRepairs.staticHandlerRepairs` (new), `HandlerBridges.instanceBridge` (new), the last of `MixinRepairs`' injector repairs | Forge 47's `MouseHandler` lambdas a screen's clicks and releases run in take `this` where vanilla's are static (LAMBDA-7), and SpongePowered's 0.8.5 refuses a static `@Inject` handler there. LAMBDA-7's bridge served a hook naming its method by one selector; Architectury's name it twice (`lambda$onPress$0` and `method_1611`), so pre-flight left out its four hooks (`onGuiMouseClicked`, `onGuiMouseClickedPost`, `onGuiMouseReleased`, `onGuiMouseReleasedPost`; the release run's Forge 47 corpus client). Not judged: a static method another installed mod's mixin adds to the target, which pre-flight doesn't see; where a selector names one beside a method that takes `this`, the bridge, which takes `this`, meets 0.8.5's exact-static check there (very unlikely). |

Terralith's own pack is read by NeoForge's mod pack reader, which the hook doesn't reach. A Fabric mod's `pack.mcmeta` that isn't JSON, or whose `pack` section can't be read, stopped NeoForge 26.x reloading its packs: NeoForge reads a mod's metadata again with its fallback section when the first read fails, outside its own handling, for a native mod's too (NeoForge 26.1.2 alone). | The test packs were Fabric jars', whose `neoforge:` sections translation leaves out (PACK-4), and no run had a native mod's pack declaring them, a `fabric:overlays` that can't be read, or a broken `pack.mcmeta`. | The plans leave upstream's hook out (NeoForge 1.21.1 to 26.3, Forge 52 and 61 to 66), and the in-game mod applies `fabric:overlays` once the game has read a pack's own overlays (`PackOverlaysMixin`, on the overlays `Pack.Metadata` is made with), the section read as upstream reads it (our own code for its format; Fabric API is Apache-2.0) and its conditions with upstream's codec and test. Core's `FabricOverlays` decides (`FabricOverlaysTest`, 8 cases): a pack declaring the platform's own conditional overlays (`neoforge:overlays` on NeoForge; Forge has none) keeps those alone; a section that can't be read is left out with one warning per pack and reason, the pack keeping its other overlays; otherwise the overlays whose conditions hold follow the pack's own. The test mod writes five packs, one like Terralith's, and reads them through `Pack.readPackMetadata` with Fabric API (`pack-overlays`, `forge-pack-overlays`, `pack-overlays-warned`), and a run has a Fabric mod whose `pack.mcmeta` isn't JSON (`server-unreadable-pack`). | NET-14 | — (Polymer's and Fabric API's race, not Fabricated's) | Fabric API 0.92's networking (`ServerPlayNetworkHandlerMixin`, its addon made at the end of the play handler's constructor), Polymer 0.5.20's early play handshake (`EarlyConnectionMagic`, its `ClientConnectionMixin`) and vanilla's play handler; the Forge 47 advanced corpus client | Past POI-4 the Forge 47 advanced client joined its world, and the server dropped it: `NullPointerException: Cannot invoke "net.fabricmc.fabric.impl.networking.server.ServerPlayNetworkAddon.handle(...ServerboundCustomPayloadPacket)" because "this.addon" is null` ("Offline lost connection"); the client's disconnect then failed in BCLib's handler ("Cannot unregister receiver while not in game!"). Polymer holds the login's `addToServer` back for its handshake, with the client already playing, and passes the client's custom payloads to its own handler until the connection's listener changes (`setPacketListener`, at its head). Vanilla's play handler sets itself as the connection's listener early in its constructor, and Fabric API makes its addon at the constructor's return, which takes a while there: the addon registers the channels the client named early, and FancyMenu's handler of that, asking for the addon not yet set, logs a caught `NullPointerException` with its stack. Vanilla handles a custom payload on the network thread, where Fabric API's hook reads the addon: one the client sent in that window found none. Carpet's redirect makes one play handler, vanilla's. All of this is the mods' code and vanilla's, alike on Fabric 1.20.1 (read in their jars; not run on Fabric); a scratch diagnostic of every payload's handler and thread showed the client's first payload (`polymer:metadata`) 24 ms after the constructor returned in a run that held, which then waited on Origins' screen (CORPUS-66). | The test mods run without Polymer's early handshake. | Not Fabricated's: no change. It struck one of two local runs. |

Its values are typed `Object`, so that no bridge method casts the holder a mod puts. Forge's registry callbacks (`GameData.PointOfInterestTypeCallbacks`, as types are added and cleared) write Forge's map itself, which the view reads, and nothing else in Forge 47 or the game names the field (read in 47.4.28's classes). POI-3's case doesn't arise on Forge 47, whose `registerBlockStates` writes nothing. The Forge 47 test mod's `ForgePoiHolderTest` puts a barrel's state with the fisherman's holder, as BCLib does, and reads it back through `forState` (`forge-legacy-poi-holder`). | BIOME-6 | P0 on Forge 47 with BCLib 3.0.17 and BetterEnd 4.0.13 (the advanced corpus server, and the client's integrated server, stopped creating their worlds once MODIFIER-3 and JAVA-1 let them load); on Forge 47 for a Fabric mod relying on what a Fabric API module Fabricated ships natively opens through its access widener | Forge 47's in-game mod: `mc/forge-legacy/1.20.1/src/fabricapi/accesstransformer.cfg` (new), which `mc/platform-forge-legacy.gradle` puts in its jar | `IllegalAccessError: Update to non-static final field net.minecraft.world.level.biome.BiomeGenerationSettings.f_186648_ attempted from a different method (bclib_setFeatureSet) than the initializer method <init>`, from BetterEnd's `EndFeatures.addBiomeFeatures` through BCLib's `BiomeAPI.addBiomeFeature`, as the world's generator settings were finalized. BCLib's accessor sets `featureSet`, `flowerFeatures` and `carvers` without `@Mutable` (only its `features` setter has it). On Fabric, Fabric API's biome module's access widener makes `BiomeGenerationSettings`' four fields mutable, and Fabric Loader applies a mod's widener to the whole game; Forge 52 to 66 pass the module through, its widener converted as any Fabric mod's, and BCLib's entrypoints run there. Forge 47 ships the module natively, without upstream's widener, whose climate field Forge 47's coremod needs private (#135). | The native modules' checks are of what they do, not of what upstream's wideners open for other mods. | This change: Forge 47's in-game mod has an access transformer with what upstream's wideners of the natively shipped modules open, in Forge 47's names, as core's converter makes them of a Fabric mod's widener (read against Forge 47.4.28's classes, every target found). Of the seven native modules only `fabric-biome-api-v1` (13.0.15) and `fabric-particles-v1` (1.1.3) have a widener: the biome's generation and spawn settings, `BiomeGenerationSettings`', `BiomeSpecialEffects`' and `MobSpawnSettings`' fields made public and mutable, and the constructors of the climate settings, the spawn cost and the particle engine's sprite set made public. Left out: `Biome`'s climate field, which Forge 47's `field_to_method.js` reads through `getModifiedClimateSettings` and needs private (CI's `forge-legacy-biome-coremod` checks the coremod applies). Forge 47's other coremods redirect none of the rest, and its own access transformer opens only `Biome$ClimateSettings` of them, and these classes' builders. The file isn't in `src/main/resources`, whose access transformer ModDevGradle applies to the game the in-game mod compiles against, which needs none of it. The Forge 47 test mod's `ForgeWidenedAccessTest` sets plains' feature set through an accessor without `@Mutable`, as BCLib does (`forge-legacy-widened-access`). Verified locally on Forge 47, with MODIFIER-3, JAVA-1 and REMAP-5: the advanced server starts, BCLib's and BetterEnd's entrypoints running, 96 checks passing and none failing; the advanced client's integrated server creates its world and then stops placing a structure's barrel (POI-4, open); the server part's seven runs and the client part's pass, the new check passing in the `server`, `server-fapi`, `server-standins` and `server-ffapi` runs. |
| REMAP-5 | P1 on Forge 47 clients with Bobby 5.0 (its client entrypoint failed: the advanced corpus client); on Forge 47 for a selector naming a record component's field or accessor by its intermediary name without its owner | `TokenRemapper.remap` (a member token's descriptor) | Bobby's client entrypoint threw `NoSuchMethodError: 'void de.johni0702.minecraft.bobby.mixin.ValidatingIntSliderCallbacksAccessor.setMaxInclusive(int)'`: pre-flight had left the accessor out, "@Accessor target field comp_594 does not exist in net.minecraft.client.OptionInstance$IntRange". Intermediary gives a record component's field and its accessor method one name (`comp_594`), and SRG names them apart (`f_231640_`, `m_214118_`), so the name alone maps to two and was kept as it was; Bobby's refmap names the field `comp_594:I`, without its owner. Mojang's names, which Forge 52 to 66 and NeoForge run with, name both alike. | The remap tests mapped each `comp_N` to one name. | This change: where a member's intermediary name maps to members the runtime names apart, the descriptor right after it in the token (`name:desc` for a field, `name(desc)ret` for a method) picks the member, read in intermediary names before the class names are mapped; where that doesn't tell them apart either (`comp_593:I`, a field of two records in Forge 47's table), the name stays as it was. `RemapTest.aSelectorsDescriptorTellsApartMembersIntermediaryNamesAlikeAndTheRuntimeDoesnt` checks a field and a method of one name, with and without an owner. Verified on a local Forge 47 advanced client: Bobby's refmap names `f_231640_:I`, pre-flight leaves none of Bobby's hooks out and its entrypoint runs; the client creates its world, then its integrated server stops on BIOME-6. |
| JAVA-1 | P0 on Forge 47 servers with Ledger 1.3.18 (the fifth extended round's advanced corpus server); on every platform for a Fabric mod built for a newer Java than the game runs on | `ModTranslator.newerJavaMixin` and `newerJavaEntrypoint` (new) | The server stopped as it bootstrapped: `UnsupportedClassVersionError: net/minecraft/world/entity/LivingEntity has been compiled by a more recent version of the Java Runtime (class file version 65.0)`, on Forge 47's Java 17. Ledger's 1.20.1 jar is built for Java 21 and declares no `depends.java` (which the resolver checks against its built-in `java` mod, as Fabric Loader does). Mixin raises a target's class version to its mixin's: Mixin 0.8.5 logged "ledger.mixins.json:entities.LivingEntityMixin: Class version 65 required is higher than the class version supported by the current version of Mixin" at debug level and applied the mixin, and the JVM refused the class it made. No Fabricated repair writes a newer class version. | No test mod or corpus mod before it was of a newer Java than the game's. | This change: a Fabric mod with an entrypoint class, or a mixin in a config without a plugin, of a newer Java than the game's isn't loaded, with the reason ("Built for a newer Java: its entrypoint com.github.quiltservertools.ledger.Ledger is a class of Java 21, which this game's Java 17 can't load") and the fix (its build for the game's Java, or the game on the Java the mod needs). Class versions are read, never changed. A config's mixins are read only where no plugin chooses them: lattice 1.3.0, nested in Axiom, is built for Java 17 and carries Java 21 mixins its plugin applies only on 1.20.5 and later, and the first build of the change disabled it, which failed Axiom's entrypoint. The translation cache's key holds the game's Java. `PipelineTest.aModWhoseMixinsAreOfANewerJavaThanTheGamesIsDisabled` checks a newer mixin and a newer entrypoint (disabled) and a newer mixin under a plugin (loaded). Verified on a local Forge 47 advanced server: Ledger isn't loaded, with that reason, and the server loads its registries and creates its world, then stops on BIOME-6; on the advanced client lattice and Axiom load. |

Mixin merges a synthetic method of an accessor mixin into its target as the mixin's other methods (`attachUniqueMethod` passes a public one to `attachMethod`); a method that is neither an accessor nor synthetic makes it an interface mixin, which refuses a class target (`MixinInfo.getVariant`, alike in SpongePowered's 0.8.5 and 0.8.7 and Fabric's 0.16.5 and 0.17.4): the first build of the change stopped the server so, "@Mixin target type mismatch: net.minecraft.world.level.chunk.ChunkGenerator is not an interface", on lithostitched's accessor. NeoForge 21.1 neither keeps the list filled nor needs a bridge: it builds the HUD from `GuiLayerManager`s, which aren't `LayeredDraw`s, and leaves `Gui.layers` empty and unused, so the constructor declares no `LayeredDraw` local and pre-flight leaves Apoli's hook out there ("captures the local variable of type LayeredDraw, which <init> in Gui does not declare", the fourth round's 1.21.1 advanced client), and Apoli's bars aren't drawn (HUD-7). Read with a probe of the registry's maps: `minecraft:point_of_interest_type`, id 7, the leatherworker's `PoiType`, which `names` holds by identity but neither `names`' nor `owners`' inverse finds, its matching states now with Amendments' liquid and dye cauldrons. Forge keeps a registry's values in Guava `HashBiMap`s keyed by their equality (`ids`, `names`, `keys`, `owners`; read in Forge 47.4.28's, 52.1.21's, 61.2.4's, 64.1.5's and 66.1.0's classes), where vanilla's `MappedRegistry` keys them by identity: a record's hash follows its components, its entries stay in the buckets the old hash gave them, and every lookup by the value misses (`getKey`, `getResourceKey` and `getID`, to which Forge's `NamespacedWrapper` hands vanilla's lookups, and the snapshot's). A map in which two values are now equal, which Forge's maps can't hold (REG-13), is left as it is; a value no mod changed is found as before. Lookups by such a value between its change and the first snapshot still miss. Verified on a local Forge 52 server with the advanced corpus: the world's data is saved, where the snapshot failed, and the server stops next creating its levels on ACC-6 (open); the advanced client's integrated server gets as far. | CI-12 | P3 (CI only) | `ci/corpus-causes.sh` | The fourth round's Forge 47 advanced client's digest of what stopped it ended on `ClassNotFoundException: com.llamalad7.mixinextras.MixinExtrasBootstrap`, `debug.log`'s last exception chain: Fabricated's probe of the MixinExtras version on Forge's layers, which fails as expected and is logged at debug level. Then Fabricated's report, the logs, and the console's earlier chains. A chain takes in the JVM's `Exception in thread` and a suppressed exception's indented causes, and leaves out exceptions logged at DEBUG or TRACE. `test_corpus_causes` (new) runs it on a game folder as the harness leaves it. Checked by hand on folders built from the fourth round's Forge 47, 52 and 66 job logs (with mawk and gawk): the Forge 47 client's digest opens on Mixin's failure, "Found 4 candidate variables" its innermost cause, and the probe is gone; the crash reports' digests are as before. | PRE-28 | P0 on Forge 52 clients with Cobblemon 1.8.4 (the advanced corpus client); wherever SpongePowered's Mixin runs (Forge 47, 52 and 61 to 66), for a mixin whose field initialiser branches or loads or stores an array element | `Initialisers` (new), `MixinRepairs.repairs`, `MixinPreflight.checkInitialiser` (new), `MixinCompat.restrictsInitialisers` (new) | The fourth extended round's Forge 52 advanced client stopped as `ItemRenderer` loaded: "Mixin apply failed mixins.cobblemon-common.json:client.ItemRendererMixin ... InvalidMixinException: Unsupported JUMP opcode in initialiser". The mixin sets a field to `Cobblemon.implementation != null && Cobblemon.implementation.getModAPI() == ModAPI.FABRIC ? "fabric_resource" : "standalone"`. Mixin merges a mixin's field initialisers, the part of its constructor after the delegate call on lines outside the constructor's own (`Constructor.getRange`, `InsnRange.apply`), into each of the target's constructors. SpongePowered's releases refuse a jump there as they merge it, copy none of its labels (a switch there would name none) and refuse an array element's load or store ("Cannot handle AASTORE opcode (0x53) in class initialiser"): `Initialiser` in 0.8.6 and 0.8.7, `MixinApplicatorStandard.getInitialiser` and `injectInitialiser` in 0.8.5, read in their classes. Fabric's Mixin (0.16.4 to 0.17.4's sources) copies the labels and takes all three. Cobblemon's `client.ModelLoaderMixin`, a field `Arrays.asList("egg", "tm")`, has an array's stores there. | Pre-flight didn't read mixins' initialisers; no test had one that branches or stores into an array. | This change: where SpongePowered's Mixin runs (`MixinCompat.restrictsInitialisers`), `Initialisers` finds the initialiser as Mixin does and moves the value of each field initialiser it refuses into a private `@Unique` method of the mixin, `fabricated$init$<field>`, which the initialiser calls (`this.f = this.fabricated$init$f()`): Mixin merges the method as the mixin's others, and the initialiser no longer branches. Only an expression on the receiver alone moves (simulated with ASM's `AnalyzerAdapter`: it doesn't reach below the receiver on the stack, stores no local or field, keeps its jumps and labels to itself), all of a mixin's refused values or none; the constructor and the methods keep their frames, expanded. A mixin it can't repair fails at class level (`MixinPreflight.checkInitialiser`), left out where it is self-contained, as Mixin would stop the game. `MixinPreflightTest.aMixinWhoseFieldInitialisersBranchOrStoreArrayElementsHasTheirValuesComputedByMethodsWhereForgesMixinRefusesThem` compiles such mixins with javac, repairs them, loads them and reads the fields' values; a value storing a pattern's local isn't moved, and its mixin is left out; a branch in a constructor's own body, which Mixin doesn't merge, and Fabric's Mixin pass. Over Cobblemon 1.8.4's classes both mixins are repaired. Verified on a local Forge 52 client with the advanced corpus: the report repairs both, Mixin applies the item renderer's into `ItemRenderer` with no failure, and the client goes past where the fourth round's stopped, to Apoli's HUD hook in `Gui`'s constructor (open); the model loader's mixin applies as models load, which that client didn't reach (UNVERIFIED in a game), as on Forge 47 and 61 to 66. |

SpongePowered's Mixin works out the locals at a point (`Locals.getLocalsAt`, read in 0.8.5 and 0.8.7) by walking the method with the frames ModLauncher hands it, as the class's bytes have them, compressed (ModLauncher 10 reads them so, read; CORPUS-79), and changes nothing at a same frame: a slot keeps the local of its last store or load. Forge 47's class, recompiled, has only same frames between the client branch and the call, so the three doubles stay among Mixin's locals there (read in Forge 47.4.28's class); vanilla's class has a chop and an append frame there instead. After an injector's repairs, its points are marked again where a repair pointed it at another constructor. | MIXCFG-1 | P0 on Forge 47 clients with ReplayMod 1.20.1 (reached once SEC-11 accepted its jar); on every platform for a mixin the platform's code can't take in a config a mod's code adds | `ModTranslator.addedConfigs` (new), `AddedMixinConfigs.added` (new) (the mixin configs a mod's code adds to Mixin itself) | ReplayMod lists two mixin configs in `fabric.mod.json` and adds its other eight from its mixin plugin, with `Mixins.addConfiguration` (LOADER-6). Pre-flight checked the configs a mod's metadata lists, so those eight's mixins reached the platform's Mixin unchecked, on every platform. On a local Forge 47 client with ReplayMod 1.20.1-2.6.23's jar accepted (SEC-11's change), the client stopped as `MouseHandler` loaded: "Critical injection failure: LVT in `MouseHandler.onScroll` has incompatible changes", for `mixins.recording.replaymod.json:MixinMouseHelper`'s `@Inject handleReplayModScroll`, which captures locals (`CAPTURE_FAILHARD`) in a method Forge 47 patches: pre-flight makes such a capture skip itself where the locals differ, and never saw this one. | No test mod adds a config from its code. | Fix in core: translation reads, in the mod's classes, the config names its calls of `Mixins.addConfiguration` give as constants (javac's `ldc` right before the call, or before its config source), as ReplayMod's launcher gives them, and pre-flight checks each such config the jar holds and the metadata doesn't list as a listed one: repairs, injectors and mixins left out, the mod disabled. The mod's call reads the config by name, as rewritten in the overlay, which wins over the jar's copy on every platform. The descriptor doesn't list it: the mod's code decides whether and when it adds a config, and the platform would add a listed one in any case (Mixin keeps one config of a name, whoever adds it first: read in SpongePowered's 0.8.5 and 0.8.7 and Fabric's 0.16.5). A call naming a config by a name its code computes, and an added config the jar doesn't hold, are reported as pre-flight warnings: their mixins aren't checked. Not judged: where NeoForge's Mixin is 0.17.4 or later, such a config applies at that behaviour version (no `[[mixins]]` entry decorates it, as on Fabric), where pre-flight takes the mod's, so a missing game class targeted in a required one is fatal there but only a warning in pre-flight. `PipelineTest.aMixinConfigTheModsCodeAddsByNameIsCheckedAsAListedOne` (a mixin left out of such a config, and another's mod disabled) and `aMixinConfigAddedByANameTheModsCodeComputesIsReportedUnchecked`, `AddedMixinConfigsTest`. Verified on a local Forge 47 client with Fabric API and ReplayMod 1.20.1-2.6.23: pre-flight checks the eight added configs and repairs their hooks where Forge's code differs, leaving none out; Mixin logs "Injection warning: LVT in `MouseHandler.onScroll` has incompatible changes" for `handleReplayModScroll` (skipped, so ReplayMod doesn't see the mouse wheel in a replay there), ReplayMod loads, and the client joins its world and exits normally (61 checks pass, none fail). |
| SEC-11 | P2 | `SafeArchive.open` (ReplayMod 1.20.1-2.6.23) | The jar has the directory entry `assets/` twice, both declaring no content, and no other name twice. SafeArchive refused any repeated name, so ReplayMod was rejected on both Forge 47 advanced corpus runs ("unsafe or corrupt archive: Duplicate entry 'assets/'", extended run 42682675827). | The duplicate check was tested with files only. | Fix: a directory's entry (a name ending in `/`) repeated, with every copy declaring no content, is accepted, once. Under ARCHITECTURE.md §14's threat model it is harmless: a repeated name matters where two readers could take different copies, and a directory has nothing to take; nothing is extracted under an entry's name; `SafeArchive.read` returns nothing for a directory; and every copy still counts against the entry limit. A repeated file, empty or not, and a repeated directory entry declaring content stay refused. `SafeArchiveTest.acceptsADirectorysEmptyEntryRepeated` and `stillRejectsARepeatedFileOrADirectoryEntryWithContent`; ReplayMod 1.20.1-2.6.23's jar opens, and it loads on a local Forge 47 client with MIXCFG-1's fix, which joins its world. |
| PLAT-7 | P0 on Forge 47 with Origins 1.10.2 (reached once MULTI-2 let it load); wherever the platform's MixinExtras starts from a config of that name | `PlatformLibraries.onlyLibraryClasses`, `CandidateScanner` (a platform library's own jar under another id) | Origins nests, through Apoli and AdditionalEntityAttributes, MixinExtras 0.2.0-beta.6's Fabric jar, whose metadata Loom generated: its id, `com_github_llamalad7_mixinextras`, isn't one of those the platform provides, so it loaded as a Fabric mod. Its classes were left out for the platform's MixinExtras, but its `mixinextras.init.mixins.json`, whose plugin is in MixinExtras' Fabric package, has the name of the in-game mod's mixinextras-forge init config: Mixin registered the copy's first and took the platform's for it (`Config.create` keeps configs by name), and with the copy's config unlisted it still read the copy's file by that name. The plugin class was gone, so MixinExtras never started, and a local Forge 47 server with Origins stopped applying Fabric API's `MobEntityMixin`, a `@ModifyArg` with a `@Local` argument ("targets a method with an invalid signature"). | Bundled copies of the platform's libraries were known by their ids, and no test had one under a generated id with a mixin config. | Fix: a jar whose classes are all in packages of a library the platform provides (outside the families it ships in part: LWJGL, Netty, Log4j) and which declares mixin configs, which can only name those classes, is that library's own jar, whatever its id, and isn't loaded, as one with a known id isn't. Without a mixin config such a jar still loads with its classes left out (GSON-1's shading test jar). `CandidateScannerTest.aCopyOfAPlatformLibraryWithMixinConfigsIsNotLoaded`. On a local Forge 47 server with Origins, MixinExtras 0.5.5 then starts and `MobEntityMixin` applies; the server stops next on Apoli (`roadmap.md`, *Compatibility baseline*). |

| PRE-22 | P0 on Forge 64 to 66 clients with ShulkerBoxTooltip (the corpus client, as the test mod's menu opens); on Forge 47 and 61 to 66 for a mixin's MixinExtras wrappers sharing locals, one of them wrapping a long or double | `ArgumentLocals` (new), `MixinPreflight.checkArgumentLocals` (new), `MixinCompat.overlapsWideArguments` (new) | The Forge 64 to 66 corpus clients crashed as the test mod's menu opened (extended run 42682670119, and the baseline runs): "VerifyError: Bad local variable type ... BundleMouseActions.onMouseScrolled(DDILnet/minecraft/world/item/ItemStack;)Z @130: aload ... Type double_2nd (current frame, locals[13]) is not assignable to reference type". ShulkerBoxTooltip 5.4.3's `BundleMouseActionsMixin` (read in its jar) has three `@WrapOperation`s into that method sharing two locals through `@Share`, in this order: of `getNumberOfItemsToShow(ItemStack)`, `toggleSelectedBundleItem(ItemStack, int, int)` and `getNextScrollWheelSelection(double, int, int)`. Mixin keeps the locals it stored one injector's values in for the next injector into the method (`Target.generateArgMap`). SpongePowered's Mixin (0.8.5 and 0.8.7 read) puts a long or double in one of them and in the slot after it without checking that slot is one of them too (`Target.allocateArgMapLocal`); Fabric's Mixin checks it (0.16.4 and 0.17.4 read). Forge 66's method uses slots 0 to 11. The first wrapper's stack went to 12, MixinExtras then allocated the shared locals (13, 14), the second's four values went to 12, 15, 16 and 17, and the third's double to 12 and 13, over a shared local: the bytecode the error prints. Fabricated's changes to the mixin are not the cause: LVT-1 drops entries of the mixin's own debug tables, and the bad frame is in the target method, which Mixin wrote. | Pre-flight followed Fabric's Mixin, and no test had wrappers sharing locals around a long or double. | Fix in core: where the running Mixin is SpongePowered's, pre-flight follows how it allocates the locals of a mixin's MixinExtras wrappers into each method (in Mixin's order, with the locals their sugar takes after each call). A wrapper whose long or double would go over one of the mixin's shared locals fails as an injector Mixin can't apply: left out in a self-contained mixin, with the injectors sharing locals with it (SHARE-1); otherwise the mod is disabled. Not judged: a method the mixin also stores values in earlier (`@Redirect`, `@ModifyArg(s)`, `@ModifyReceiver`), points pre-flight can't place, other mixins' wrappers into the method. `MixinPreflightTest.aWrappersLongOrDoubleThatMixinWouldStoreOverASharedLocalFails` fails without it; `TranslateHelpersTest`. Pre-flight over ShulkerBoxTooltip's jar and Forge 66's classes leaves out the three hooks, so scrolling a bundle in its preview doesn't work on Forge. Verified on a local Forge 66 client with Fabric API and ShulkerBoxTooltip 5.4.3: Mixin applies the rest of the mixin to `BundleMouseActions` as the test mod's menu opens, the menu check passes with no `VerifyError`, and the client exits normally. |

GitHub's own actions (`actions/*`) are taken by their major tags. | A tag resolves when the job starts; nothing records which commit ran. | This change: the three uses name the commit `v4` named then (v4.4.3's, `ed408507`), with the version beside it; a newer version is a change to the workflow. |

On Windows the delete fails, the file being open; on Linux the open jar stays readable; whether Forge opens it again by its path in that launch is UNVERIFIED. | No launch test runs seven launches in one game directory; the cache's tests use one instance a launch. | The dependency locator records the language provider's use through the pipeline's cache, as it does the in-game mod's (`NestedJars.extract(NestedJars.LANG_PROVIDER, cache)`; a development workspace has none, which is logged at debug). Verified: a Forge 52 server run with Fabric API loads the language provider and the Fabric mods as before, and the cache index records the jar's use on that launch. |
| SEC-6 | P1 (a hostile jar) where the platform runs ASM 9.8 or older (NeoForge 21.11.45, which resolves 9.8) | `Pipeline` (a mod's translation, and another jar's classes remapped for pre-flight), `ClassBytesSource.readNode`, `ClassIndex`, `LinkageChecker`, `LinkageAnalysis` | One class file stopped the game while it discovered mods: ASM before 9.9 allocates an unknown attribute's content at the length the class gives it, before reading it, so a 72-byte class whose attribute says `Integer.MAX_VALUE` bytes throws `OutOfMemoryError` ("Requested array size exceeds VM limit", on any heap; a length just under 2 GiB exhausts a 2 GiB heap the same way). Every catch around a mod took runtime exceptions and stack overflows only, so the error left the mod's translation (on 1.21.x, which remaps every class), another mod's pre-flight (which reads every jar's mixins) and the linkage check (26.x), and the dependency locator rethrows a VM error. ASM 9.9 and later, and the JVM, refuse such a class with an exception. ASM 9.7 fails as 9.8 does. | The hostile classes tested nested annotations (SEC-3); ASM's own allocations weren't considered, and only 1.21.11 of CI's NeoForge versions runs an ASM older than 9.9. | This change: an `OutOfMemoryError` reading a mod's classes is caught where a stack overflow is. It disables the mod being translated, reported as unsafe, corrupt or too large for the game's memory, and another mod's pre-flight and the linkage check read the class as unreadable (absent), as any corrupt class. A refused allocation leaves the heap as it was. `PipelineTest.aClassAskingForMoreMemoryThanAnArrayHoldsStopsNoOtherMod` (both namespaces; without the change the error ends the test JVM), `InstalledClassesTest.aMixinClassClaimingMoreThanAnArrayHoldsIsSkipped` (fails without the change). |

| FRAME-1 | — (as on Fabric) | Fabric's Mixin 0.16.4, 0.16.5 and 0.17.4 (`ClassInfo.forName`, `ClassInfo.FrameData`, `Locals.getLocalsAt`), which NeoForge 21.1, 21.11 and 26.x run; the shared test mod's recorded frames check (`RecordedFramesTest`, `RecordedFramesMixin`, new) | Forge's recorded frames check (CORPUS-79), ported to the shared test mods, failed on the NeoForge 1.21.1, 1.21.11 and 26.1.2 servers: "Found 0 candidate variables but exactly 1 is required" for its `@ModifyVariable` of the probe's `StringBuilder` after the chop, and the probe's class didn't load. Read in Fabric's Mixin sources: `ClassInfo.forName` reads a class's metadata through the service's bytecode provider with no `ClassReader` flags unless `CLASSREADER_EXPAND_FRAMES` is set (it isn't by default), so it records the frames compressed; `FrameData.size` is the frame's locals raised to the method's argument slots (`Math.max(rawSize, initialFrameSize)`; `rawSize`, without the floor, is read only by the behaviour 0.9.2 local computation, `getLocalsAt092`); `getLocalsAt` (behaviour 0.10.0 on) adds an append's recorded size and subtracts a chop's: 4 + 4, then 8 - 4, four locals where six are live. NeoForge hands Mixin the class it walks as Fabric Loader does, its frames expanded (FML 10 to 12's `ClassTransformer`, NeoForge 21.1's ModLauncher 11, Fabric Loader's `TreeTransformer.readClass`), and reads the metadata as Knot's `MixinServiceKnot.getClassNode` does, with Mixin's flags (FML's `FMLClassBytecodeProvider`, Mixin's own ModLauncher service on 21.1); Forge's ModLauncher fork hands the class compressed (CORPUS-79). On a vanilla 26.1.2 server with Fabric Loader 0.19.4 (Mixin 0.17.4) the same probe failed the same way in a mod declaring `fabricloader` >=0.19.0, as the test mod does (behaviour 0.17.1), and found the local in one declaring no lower bound (behaviour 0.9.2). | — (CORPUS-79 read Fabric's `rawSize` as the size its local computation reads) | No change to Mixin's count on NeoForge: it counts the probe's locals as Fabric does, and Forge's repair, run on NeoForge, would also change the count of every method Fabric's Mixin miscounts too (1,093 of the 14,102 methods with frames in vanilla 26.1.2's server lose a live local at some frame). What NeoForge's own classes change is FRAME-2. The check is the shared test mods' (the Forge test mods' copies are gone): its variable modifier takes the `StringBuilder` by type and ordinal, not required, so it applies nowhere where Mixin finds none instead of stopping the class, and `ci/checks.tsv` holds each platform's outcome: `recorded-frames` on Forge 61 to 66 (the repair) and Forge 47, `recorded-frames-fabric` (as on Fabric) on NeoForge. `RecordedFramesTest` (core) also walks Forge's shape expanded, as NeoForge and Fabric Loader hand it, against frames recorded compressed: 8 then 4 locals. Verified on local server runs (`ci/smoke.sh <platform> <minecraft> server server`): before, with the variable modifier by type alone and required, NeoForge 1.21.1 (70 pass, 2 fail, 4 skip), 1.21.11 and 26.1.2 (71 pass, 2 fail, 3 skip each) failed `recorded-frames` and `no-failed`; after, NeoForge 1.21.1 (72 pass, 4 skip), 1.21.11 and 26.1.2 (73 pass, 3 skip), Forge 61 and 64 (90 pass, 2 skip) and Forge 47 (95 pass, 2 skip) failed nothing, the Forge branches synced and their copies gone. NeoForge 26.1.2's whole server part (its nine runs, the apply-failure runs among them) passed. |
| FLUID-4 | P0 on Forge 52 clients with Fabric API's jar (a world with lava, or a Fabric fluid without an overlay, in view) | Upstream's `FluidRendererMixin.modSpriteArray` (fabric-rendering-fluids-v1 0.116, passed through); `LiquidBlockRendererSpritesMixin` (Forge 52, new) | Once fabric-rendering-v1 loaded on Forge 52's client (COLOR-3), the client crashed meshing the first lava it saw: `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: Index 2 out of bounds for length 2` in `LiquidBlockRenderer.tesselate`, under Fabric API's fluid rendering. Forge 52 takes a fluid's still, flowing and overlay sprites from its fluid type (`ForgeHooksClient.getFluidSprites`) and reads the third for a side's overlay; upstream's hook puts the fluid render handler's sprites in their place, two for a fluid without an overlay. | fabric-rendering-v1 was off on Forge 52's client (COLOR-3), and Fabric's fluid renderer with it. | Just after upstream's hook, an array without the overlay slot gets an empty one, which Forge reads as no overlay. The hook sits at upstream's point, the neighbour fluid check `tesselate` always makes, and the mixin has a higher priority than upstream's, so it applies after upstream's hook there. Each half was needed: at the method's first neighbour occlusion check, made only for a bottom face it draws, a fluid not drawing its bottom face kept upstream's array; applied first (as one CI client's transformer audit showed), upstream's array replaced the fixed one. Verified in the Forge 52 client runs, and in the debug log's mixing order: the client meshes its world and stops normally. |

| SEC-5 | P3 | `CandidateScanner` (nested jars) | Nesting was bounded per top-level jar (depth 8, 1024 jars, 512 MiB of nested jars), not across all of them: many hostile jars could each extract that much to the cache. The 1 MiB cap on metadata applies after reading the entry, which since SEC-4 is bounded by its declared size and the compression ratio. Task 6 closed it as won't fix, which the beta's stability bar doesn't allow (item 4: the limits on nested jars hold across all the jars of a launch). | The scanner's tests gave it one top-level jar at a time. | Task 7: all the top-level jars of a launch share one budget, eight top-level jars' worth (8192 nested jars, and 4 GiB with the default `maxTotalUncompressedBytes`), and each keeps its own; a nested jar past either is rejected, the report naming the limit, and takes nothing from either. A top-level jar's budget is already far above any mod's: Fabric API, which nests the most, nests one jar per module, fewer than a hundred. `CandidateScannerTest.nestedJarsOfAllTopLevelJarsShareTheLaunchsBudget`: of fourteen jars, each within its own budget, the launch's takes twelve libraries and rejects the other two, whose jars still load. |

Extended run 42524794672 on 26.1.2: both clients with the Fabric builds of Sodium and Iris stopped "out of its world, 0 loaded", each with its terrain block at the chest's place; the same builds passed it in extended run 42522554523 (10 and 42 loaded), and the integrated server's check, through the same mixin, passed in both. | The check passed wherever the world had another block entity near spawn, as most have; the two test mods place their blocks by the player and were written apart. | Task 6: the chest goes two blocks behind the player, where the renderer API test mod places nothing. The cause was read in that run's log (the two clients' block positions); the PR run and the extended run on #316's last commit run the check on every client. Earlier misses on clients with that test mod (run 673's Sodium client on 26.2, PROVIDER-19's on 26.1.2) may be the same; their logs kept no line to tell. |
| PERF-4 | P3 (Forge 47 clients with a Fabric block model that throws) | Indigo's `TerrainRenderContext.tessellateBlock` (Fabric API 0.92's, upstream code); Forge 47's `FabricBlockRendering` (rule 8) | A Fabric model's first throw in a chunk section held its worker about 1.4 s in R5's run (41423804163) before rule 8's fallback drew the block. Indigo catches the throw and builds vanilla's crash report for it before rethrowing it (read in its 1.20.1 source), and a crash report gathers the system's details, its hardware's among them; Fabricated's guard, around Fabricated's call into Indigo, catches what Indigo rethrows, the cost already paid. On Forge 61 to 66 the guard is on Indigo's own call into the model (`IndigoTerrainMixin`, `IndigoBlockRendererMixin` and their siblings), before Indigo's catch. | Measured once, in R5's run; no check times a throw. | Decided (task 6): no change. The cost comes with a model that throws, a mod's bug that stops the game on Fabric, and rule 8 stops calling that model's class after its first throw, so it is paid once per class and launch, on a worker rather than the render thread; a model wrapping a broken one, whose class rule 8 can't tell from its frame, pays it at each rebuild of its section. Moving the guard onto Indigo's call into the model, as on Forge 61 to 66, takes mixins into Indigo's 1.20.1 render contexts: after the beta. |

Extended run 42522554523, warm: the mappings phase 9 to 13 ms on 1.21.x; the in-game mod phase 19 to 23 ms on 1.21.1 (44 to 59 before; on 1.21.11 and 26.x it stayed within the runners' spread, so there the jar's read was not most of it); the vanilla game phase 26 to 49 ms (26.3's 93 to 96 down to 34 to 39). The NeoForge mods' nested jars, its fourth suspect, are PERF-3. |
| PERF-1 | P3 | `PacketEncoderContextMixin`, `PacketDecoderContextMixin`, `GenericPacketSplitterContextMixin` (26.x) | Each wraps every packet's encoding or decoding (vanilla's too) with `@WrapMethod`, which allocates the `Operation`'s argument array, a `ScopedValue` carrier and a lambda per packet. It was unmeasured. | — | No change (task 6): a microbenchmark of the same shape (an `Operation` with its argument array, a `ScopedValue` binding and a lambda around a plain call; JDK 21, four rounds of 20 million calls) costs about 15 to 20 ns and 56 bytes a packet more than the plain call (about 2 ns, nothing allocated). A server sending 20,000 packets a second spends about 0.4 ms a second on it and allocates about 1 MB a second of objects that die young, against a tick's 50 ms. Not measured in a game under load: CI has none. |
| P3-2 | P3 | `LootBridge` | One global reload manager: two overlapping reloads can label a loot table with the wrong source. | — | Won't fix (task 6): reloads overlap only when a command queued during one starts another (vanilla's `reloadResources` waits on the server thread with `managedBlock`, which runs the queued tasks), and the effect is a table's `LootTableSource` label in Fabric's loot events. A key per reload needs a mixin into each version's table parsing lambda. |

The Fabric builds are pinned the same way (`ci_fabric_renderers`): on 1.21.11 and 26.x the pairs that passed in Forge's extended run 41848003908, which on 1.21.11, 26.1.2 and 26.2 are the builds that passed in extended run 42522554523 too; 1.21.1, whose ids no kept log names, takes the newest until a run prints them. | CI-3 | P3 (CI only) | The client checks of `.github/workflows/build.yml` and `ci/neoforge/smoke-client.sh`, which read `logs/latest.log` | A client run crossing 00:00 UTC has its log rotated by log4j: the lines before midnight move to `logs/<date>-1.log.gz`, and their checks fail with the lines missing (#230's run 40790221936 on 26.1.2: `model deserializer OK`, `neoforge blockstate type OK`, `key mapping OK`, all in the rotated file). | Only a run crossing midnight UTC rotates the log. | After the game exits, the smoke script puts the run's rotated logs (oldest first) in front of `latest.log`, for its own checks and the workflow's; the run directory is new each run, so they are this run's. Checked on a run directory holding a rotated log beside `latest.log` (the debug log's rotations left out): the checks read both. The Forge branches' scripts get the same in their task 6 change. |
| PLAT-2 | P3 (no case seen) | core `PlatformLibraries`, `PackageGuard` | The package guard took every package under `org/lwjgl/`, `io/netty/` and `org/apache/logging/log4j/` for the platform's, but the game ships only some of those libraries' modules (LWJGL's bindings it uses, Netty's transport and codecs, Log4j's API and core): a mod nesting another (LWJGL's NanoVG, Netty's HTTP codec) lost it and failed with `ClassNotFoundException`, as PLAT-1 did for night-config's JSON module. | No test or corpus mod nests such a module; the prefixes were whole families from the start. | In those three families a mod's package is the platform's only where the platform's loaders have one of its classes (`PlatformLibraries.isProvided` with the platform's class source). The packages the loader reads from its layers can't decide it: on FML 10 and later the game's LWJGL is on no module layer. A class the platform has but can't read still counts as the platform's, so a split package stays impossible. Unit tests (`PlatformLibrariesTest`, `PipelineTest.aLibrarysModuleThePlatformLacksStaysInTheMod`); every run of PR run 42521740905 loaded as before. |
| FC-6 | P3 | `TranslationCache.store`, `lookup` and the index | Two launches sharing a game directory (a client and a server from one folder, two clients started together) that both missed an entry both translated it, and the second's store deleted, then replaced, the files the first's game was already using. The last launch to save the index also dropped what the others had recorded, which then counted toward cleaning their entries. | Each test used one cache instance at a time. | A store keeps an entry already there with the same files, each intact, and replaces one with other or damaged files by moving it aside in one step and deleting it there; a lookup sets a damaged entry aside only if its `entry.json` is still the one it read. The index is saved under a lock (`index.lock`, waiting up to 5 s, else as before), after merging what other launches recorded since this one read it. `TranslationCacheTest` stores the same entry from two instances, replaces other and damaged entries, and saves two launches' index in turn. |
| FC-5 | P3 | `TranslationCache.sha256` (the hash memo), `verifyIntegrity` | Hashes are memoised by file metadata. On Windows the stamp was size and modification time only (it has no change time or file key), and `entry.json` itself isn't hashed. It needed a decision on cost: hash every translated jar on each launch, or trust metadata. | — | Decided (task 6): the memo trusts a file's metadata, as the game trusts the mods folder, rather than read every translated jar again on each launch. On Windows the stamp adds the creation time, which a jar copied in as a new file gets (one written over the old file in place may keep the old one's; UNVERIFIED there, as CI runs on Linux). A damaged `entry.json` fails to parse and its entry is rebuilt. |

| CORPUS-104 | P2 on Forge 47 to 66 clients with Fabric API, for a Fabric mod's built-in pack enabled by default (by the code); P3 for the log lines on the Forge 61 and 64 clients with Artifacts or Terralith (the corpus clients) | `fabric-resource-loader`'s hook at the return of `Options.load` (v1's `OptionsMixin.onLoad`, through `DefaultResourcePackStorage.process`; v0's `GameOptionsMixin.onLoad` in Fabric API 0.92); Forge's client, which creates the options before `ClientModLoader.begin` runs the mods' registry events; `FabricDefaultPacks` (new, on both Forge branches) | The Forge 64 corpus client logged `JsonParseException: Unknown resource condition key: artifacts:config_value; Unknown resource condition key: fabric:not; ...`, and the same for `terralith:config`, as it read those mods' pack metadata (#278's extended run 41755238645). Read in Fabric API's and Forge's 26.1.2 and 1.20.1 sources: the hook reads every Fabric mod's pack and built-in pack, switches on a built-in pack registered as enabled by default the first time it is offered, and keeps a file of the packs it offered, so a pack the player turns off stays off. On Fabric the options load after the Fabric mods' entrypoints. Forge creates them before its mod loading, which runs the entrypoints. So the hook found no built-in pack, switched none on and dropped every pack it had kept, and it read the mods' packs before their condition types were registered (the log lines). The game's own pack discovery read those packs later, without the error, so their overlays applied. NeoForge's native resource loader does this itself after the entrypoints (PACK-6). | No test mod registered a built-in pack enabled by default, and no check asked which packs the game selected; the log lines were in the informational corpus runs only. | forge #291 (Forge 61 to 66): the plans leave `OptionsMixin#onLoad` out, and `FabricDefaultPacks` runs upstream's `DefaultResourcePackStorage.process` once the entrypoints have run, still in Forge's mod loading and before the game selects its packs from the options. forge-legacy #293 (Forge 47): the plan leaves `GameOptionsMixin#onLoad` out, and `FabricDefaultPacks` does that hook's work in Forge 47's names, as Fabric API 0.92 has it only inside the hook: the same file of offered packs, and the Fabric mods' own packs kept on. The test mods register a built-in pack enabled by default from their client entrypoints, and the client smoke with Fabric API asks that the game selected it. Verified in extended runs 41782561336 (Forge 61 to 66: the check passes, and the corpus clients log no unreadable pack and reach their worlds) and 41782464279 (Forge 47: the check passes, and the corpus client reaches its world, with no error from the processing). |
| PROVIDER-13 | P0 for packs with Forgified Fabric API on Forge 47, once coexisting with it: the server stopped loading data packs | `NativeModules.modules` (new), `NativeModules.standDown`, `Pipeline.providedElsewhere`, `Pipeline.Result.alsoProvidedElsewhere` (new), the locators | With coexisting tried, the Forge 47 server with Forgified Fabric API 0.92.6 stopped as the data packs' tags loaded (extended run 41578329787's informational "server with Forgified Fabric API" step): "NoSuchMethodError: FuelRegistryImpl.apply(Map)" from Fabricated's fuel hook (`FabricFuels`, FUEL-1), and "LootTable cannot be cast to FabricLootSupplier". Two gaps in the provider policy: only the plan's modules could count as provided, so the parts of the in-game mod serving modules Fabricated passes through (fabric-content-registries-v0's fuel and brewing hooks, the renderer seams of fabric-renderer-api-v1) stayed beside the provider's copy, and Forgified Fabric API's own fuel registry has no `apply`; and a mixin config in a provided module's package stood down whole, with the mixins in its sub-package of a module the provider lacks (Forge 47's loot config, in fabric-loot-api-v2's package, has fabric-loot-tables-v1's table mixins, and Forgified Fabric API 1.20.1 has no fabric-loot-tables-v1), which Fabricated's copy of that module needs. | The stand-in provider's gate provides plan modules only, with no sub-package config, and the real one's run was refused until 3b8bdf4. | #253: a module beyond the plan that the data files name (`native-modules.json`, `mixin-modules.json`) counts as provided too, for the stand-down and for the in-game mod's bridges (`FabricatedState.providedElsewhere`); a config in a provided module's package keeps the mixins of a sub-package whose module isn't provided, and stands down whole only when none is left or its plugin's package leaves (`NativeModulesTest.aConfigOfAProvidedModuleKeepsTheMixinsOfAModuleNotProvided`, `aConfigWhosePluginLeavesStandsDownWhole`). forge-legacy #249: coexisting with Forgified Fabric API on Forge 47 (3b8bdf4), and fabric-loot-tables-v1's initializer running whenever Fabricated's copy of it does, on the provider's fabric-loot-api-v2 events. Verified in forge-legacy's extended runs 41778812320 and 41782464279: the Forge 47 server with Forgified Fabric API gets past its data packs, reaches its world and saves it. The test mod's checks that still fail there (the registry entry added event's raw id, the fuel table, the loot tables' sources, the registry aliases) meet the provider's own modules. |

Those blocks were drawn as missing models. Read in Forge's 1.20.1, Moonlight's 1.20 and Porting Lib's 1.20.1 sources: Forge's reader reads every block model's `loader` and fails the model when its own registry lacks that loader. On Fabric vanilla ignores the key, and a Fabric mod reads its own loaders through its hook into vanilla's model reader: Moonlight's for Supplementaries' loaders, Porting Lib's for its own. Forge's reader calls vanilla's first, so those hooks had run when Forge looked the loader up. | No test model named a loader Forge lacks. | forge-legacy #289: Forge reads a loader it has, and one in a Forge mod's namespace (or Forge's, or the game's), whose failure is that mod's, as without Fabricated. Any other loader, in a Fabric mod's namespace or no loaded mod's, is left to the Fabric mods' hooks, and the model is what vanilla's reader and those hooks made of it, as on Fabric. Only a model's own `loader` goes through this. The test mod ships a model naming a loader in its own namespace, which must load, and one in Forge's, which must still fail (client smoke). Verified in extended run 41776560917: the corpus client fails no Supplementaries or Create model, and logs no Forge "Model loader ... not found" but the test mod's; Porting Lib's own warning for Supplementaries' loaders shows, as it would on Fabric. |

| CORPUS-108 | P1 on Forge 47 to 66 for any Fabric mod's `RegistryEntryAddedCallback` listener on a registry Forge manages; seen on Forge 47 clients with Artifacts and EMI, JEI or REI (the corpus client) | Fabric API's `RegistryEntryAddedCallback` (`fabric-registry-sync-v0`), Forge's `NamespacedWrapper` and `ForgeRegistry`, Architectury's `ArchitecturySpawnEggItem` and Fabric `RegistrarManagerImpl.listen`; the `forge` and `forge-legacy` in-game mods' `NamespacedWrapperEntryAddedMixin`, `ForgeRegistryEntryAddedMixin` and `RegistryEntryAddedBridge` (new) | As EMI, JEI and REI listed Artifacts' creative tab, `SpawnEggItem.getDefaultType()` returned null for one of its eggs, and each failed on `NullPointerException` ("Cannot invoke "net.minecraft.world.entity.EntityType.m_245183_()" because the return value of "net.minecraft.world.item.SpawnEggItem.getDefaultType()" is null"; extended runs 41762493256 and 41770251158). JEI leaves the tab's items out ("Item Group crashed while building contents"), REI fails to collect the tab, and EMI's own plugin fails with it (CORPUS-111). Read in the sources (Architectury's `1.20`, Fabric API's `1.20.1` and `1.21.11`, MinecraftForge's `1.20.1` to `26.3`): Architectury's spawn egg takes its entity type through `RegistrySupplier.listen`, which on Fabric waits on `RegistryEntryAddedCallback` while the type isn't registered. Fabric API fires that event at the return of vanilla's `MappedRegistry.registerMapping` (`register` on 1.21.11 and 26.x). Forge's `NamespacedWrapper`, the vanilla registry of the registries Forge manages, replaces that method without calling vanilla's, and a Forge mod's entries go to Forge's registry alone, so on every Forge version the event never fired for blocks, items, entity types and the rest. Forgified Fabric API (1.20.1) patches the wrapper's method the same way. Which of Artifacts' eggs it was is UNVERIFIED; each takes its type so. | no test listened for registry entries; the alias test only reads entries back. | the event fires once an entry new to such a registry is in it, with its raw id, whether it came through vanilla's API or Forge's (`ForgeRegistry.register`, where `DeferredRegister` and `RegisterEvent` put a Forge mod's entries); a registry snapshot's load fires nothing, as on Fabric (#286 on Forge 47, #287 on Forge 61 to 66). The test mod's listener hears of an item registered each way, once with its raw id (`ForgeRegistryEntryAddedTest`, a server smoke check with Fabric API on every Forge version). Verified in the CI of #286 and #287, where the check passes, and in `forge-legacy`'s extended run 41772824375 (#286): the Forge 47 corpus client logs no `getDefaultType()` error, and EMI, JEI and REI list Artifacts' tab without one. With Forgified Fabric API providing the module (that run's informational server), the listener hears of the vanilla API's item with raw id -1 and not of the Forge API's: the provider's module, for which Fabricated's mixins stand down. |

Read in the sources (Porting Lib's `1.20.1`, MinecraftForge's `1.20.1`): Porting Lib ports Forge's drop capture to Fabric. On Forge, its capture is Forge's: Porting Lib adds `captureDrops` to `Entity` under Forge's names, and Mixin keeps Forge's methods. | CORPUS-103 | P0 on Forge 47 clients with Dripstone Fluid Lib (the corpus client); on Forge 47 for any Fabric mod's `@Inject` into a constructor that isn't at a return or at a call | Dripstone Fluid Lib's `MinecraftClientMixin` (`dripstone_fluid_lib$handleParticles`) and `ParticleManagerMixin` (its redirect of the particle reload's `forEach`); SpongePowered Mixin 0.8.5's `Injector.checkTargetForNode`; `ConstructorRepairs.constructorPointRepairs`, `ConstructorPoint` (new, core), `forge-legacy`'s `ConstructorInjectionPoint` (new) | With CORPUS-101 fixed, the Forge 47 corpus client stopped later in its first resource reload (extended run 41755235266, "Rendering overlay"): a `NullPointerException` in `ParticleEngine.m_244719_`, called through Dripstone's redirect `addReloadResults`, as `spriteSets.get` gave null. Read in Dripstone's sources (`master`): its hook into `Minecraft`'s constructor registers its particles' providers at the call of `ParticleEngine`'s constructor, shifted by 2 (just after the engine is stored), and its redirect adds reload results for those particles (the ids ending `_dripstone_lib_particle_type_hang`, `_fall` and `_splash`). Pre-flight left that hook out: SpongePowered's Mixin 0.8.5, which Forge 47 runs, takes an `@Inject` into a constructor only at a return (every other point restricts its targets to methods, and 0.8.5 has no `unsafe`), and CTOR-4's wrapper only took a hook at a call, not one shifted past it. The point's arguments carry the original point's type and shift, and the `@At` keeps its target, ordinal, opcode and slice. Forge 47's loader registers the point with Mixin before any Fabric mod's mixin is read. The point finds what the original finds, moved by the shift, and Mixin takes it anywhere in a constructor, as `unsafe` does from 0.8.6; an instance handler still goes only after the delegate call. It replaces CTOR-4's MixinExtras wrapper. On 0.8.7 such a point is marked `unsafe`, now with its shift counted (`MixinPreflightTest.anInjectJustAfterAConstructionInAConstructorIsTakenWhereMixinTakesOnlyReturns`). The forge-legacy test mod adds a hook just after `Commands`' constructor stores its dispatcher, shifted by 2, and CTOR-4's hook now goes through the point; both smoke checks need them. Verified in `forge-legacy`'s extended run 41762493256 (#281, main synced in): the Forge 47 corpus client got past its first resource reload and created its world, and the test mod's hook shifted past `Commands`' dispatcher ran on the gates and the corpus. |

Mixin's own cause (extended run 41658054089) was "Found 0 candidate variables but exactly 1 is required": Mixin 0.8.7's `Locals.getLocalsAt` gave no `PoseStack` there, though the jar's method has one in scope by its local variable table and frames. Read in Mixin 0.8.7's source: `getLocalsAt` takes each frame's change in size from the frames Mixin recorded for the class (`ClassInfo.FrameData`), recorded from the class read without expanding its frames (unless its `CLASSREADER_EXPAND_FRAMES` option is set), and it records an appending or chopping frame's size as at least the method's argument slots (`computeFrameSize`), not the locals appended or chopped. `renderScreenEffect` has four argument slots, appends three locals and chops one: Mixin counts 4 + 4, then 8 - 4, four locals where six are live, and drops the `PoseStack`. Forge's ModLauncher fork (10.2, which Forge 61 to 66 run) hands Mixin the class with its frames compressed, so nothing corrects the count. Mixin 0.8.5 (Forge 47) records the frames expanded. Fabric's Mixin counts them as 0.8.7 does from its behaviour 0.10.0 (FRAME-1); the mod works on Fabric, where vanilla's method (26.1.2's: six argument slots, the `PoseStack` and `Player` appended in one frame, no chop) is counted too high but drops no local. On Forge 65 and 66, ResourcefulLib 5 and 6 hook `submit` instead. | No test applied a mixin taking a local in a method whose frames append fewer locals than its argument slots and then chop. Methods Mixin counts right, and classes no Fabric mod's mixin targets, keep its frames. `RecordedFramesTest` covers Forge's shape (8 then 4 locals counted where there are 7 then 6, and right once recorded expanded, whether the frames walked are compressed or expanded), vanilla's shape, a chopped long, a method Mixin counts right, and the expanded frames of an instance method, a constructor with uninitialized values and a static method with every kind of argument. Forge 47's Mixin needs no repair. |
