| CORPUS-80 | P1 on Forge 47 to 66 with Farmer's Delight (refabricated), and the mods needing it (Supplementaries and Moonlight on Forge 47) | Forge 61 to 66: `EnumValuesMixins.holderClass`, `withHeldConstants` and `initReturns` (new), `ModTranslator.valuesMixin` (enum mixins into `RecipeBookType` and `SearchRecipeBookCategory`); Forge 47: `EarlyRisers` and `ModTranslator.earlyRisers` (new), `ModTranslator.processMixins` (Fabric ASM's enum additions) | On Forge 61 to 66 pre-flight disables Farmer's Delight (extended run 41632339327): its `RecipeBookTypeMixin` and `SearchRecipeBookCategoryMixin` are enum mixins, which Forge's Mixin 0.8.7 doesn't apply, and ENUM-7 didn't turn them into ordinary mixins because the mod's own classes read the constants as fields of the enums (`FDRecipeBookTypes.COOKING = RecipeBookType.FARMERSDELIGHT_COOKING`, and its recipe book screen's search category), which no mixin can add. Had it, the game would have stopped as `RecipeBookType` loaded: Forge makes that enum extensible, its `create` factory runs `init` on each constant it makes, and Forge 61 to 66's `init` calls `RecipeBookSettings.register`, which throws "This is not implemented yet" (Forge's 1.21.10 to 26.3 patches). On Forge 47 it adds its recipe book types with Fabric ASM (`ClassTinkerers.enumBuilder` in an early riser, nested Fabric ASM 2.3), which Fabricated doesn't run: `FarmersDelight`'s class initializer failed, and Supplementaries and Moonlight, which use its classes, threw `NoClassDefFoundError` (extended runs 41590139262 and 41632355968; the evidence step printed the calls). | Forge 61 to 66: ENUM-7's test disabled a mod reading a constant as a field, by design, and no test had an extensible enum whose `init` throws. Forge 47: no test had a mod using Fabric ASM, whose plugin can't run on Forge (it adds classes to Fabric's class loader; Mixin drops its config). Then ENUM-7's test took Forge 47's `RecipeBookType.init` as one to run, since it returns (`EnumValuesMixinsTest`). | Forge 61 to 66: the mod's reads of the constants as fields of the enum, and any in the code moved into the enum, read them from a holder class generated next to the mixins. Its fields are set from the enum's values by name; a constant missing there (its mixin didn't apply) fails the holder with the enum, the constant and the mixin named. Into an extensible enum whose `init` can't return, the mixin cancels `init` for the mod's constants only, which Fabric gives no such hook; other constants keep it (`PipelineTest.anEnumMixinsConstantsTheModReadsAsFieldsOfTheEnumComeFromAHolderWhereMixinAppliesNoEnumMixins`, and two `EnumValuesMixinsTest` tests that run the code). Limit: another mod reading these constants as fields (an addon, through the class tweaker's `transitive-extend-enum`) still finds none. Forge 47: Fabric ASM's config is left out. Translation reads the early risers' `enumBuilder` calls without running them and writes the constants as enum mixins of its own, which ENUM-7 turns into ordinary mixins calling Forge's `create` factories. A supplier's arguments come from a helper next to it, which runs it as the enum is initialized (`EarlyRisersTest`, `PipelineTest.fabricAsmsEarlyRisersEnumConstantsComeFromEnumMixinsOfFabricatedsOwn`). Extended run 41675005964 (after #263's sync) made the constants, and Farmer's Delight's entrypoint then failed with a `NullPointerException`: Forge's `create` runs `init` on each constant it makes, and Forge 47's `RecipeBookType.init` registers the type's tags with `RecipeBookSettings`. That class's initializer then ran while `RecipeBookType` was still initializing from `FarmersDelight`'s (`RECIPE_TYPE_COOKING = RecipeBookType.valueOf(...)`), and the mod's `RecipeBookSettingsMixin` added its type's tags there from that field, still null. The client stopped next, as Forge's `RecipeBookManager.init` initialized `RecipeBookCategories`: the meals category's supplier made an item stack of an item the failed entrypoint never registered ("No delegate exists for value air"). Fix: Forge's `init` is skipped for the mod's constants also where it reaches beyond the enum (`EnumValuesMixins.initKeepsToEnum`, new). Fabric gives them no such hook, and the mod adds its tags itself; an `init` keeping to the enum (Forge 47's `MobCategory`'s, filling the map its codec reads) still runs. Verified on Forge 61 to 66 in extended run 41682699918, where Farmer's Delight loaded and ran its entrypoints on the Forge 61, 64, 65 and 66 corpus servers and clients, and every server started; on Forge 47 in extended run 41703581030, after #267, with CORPUS-37's fix: Farmer's Delight's entrypoints ran on the corpus server and client, Supplementaries and Moonlight loaded on both, and the client got past `RecipeBookManager.init`. |

On Forge the handler is Balm 7.3.44's `FabricCropBlockMixin.getGrowthSpeedCaptureLocals` (read in its source): it modifies the `BlockState` at the first call of `BlockState.is(Block)`, vanilla's farmland check inside the loop; Forge's build asks `canSustainPlant` there, so the first call is after the loop, where the state's local is gone, though the method has one (the method-wide check passed it). | No test had a variable modifier picking its local by its type alone where the method has no such local, or none in scope at its point; pre-flight checked only that such a method stores some local. | Fix: a variable modifier picking its local by its type alone (no ordinal, index, name or `argsOnly`), whose methods have no local of that type by their local variable tables, arguments and stores, is treated as Mixin will treat it: where Mixin would stop on it, it is left out (or its mod disabled) with the reason; where Mixin would inject nothing, pre-flight warns. `MixinPreflightTest.aVariableModifierByTypeFailsWhereTheMethodHasNoLocalOfThatType` fails without it. Verified on NeoForge in extended run 41536167292 (on `32c8c5d`): the 1.21.1 corpus client stayed in its world and exited normally, where it had crashed on Puzzles Lib's hook. Forge's: pre-flight also judges the points a call or field `@At` picks, in methods with a local variable table (as Forge's patched methods have): no argument or table entry of the type there, and each slot that held one stored to again since, every instruction from that store to the point covered by the table, so that Mixin's locals there (`Locals.getLocalsAt`) can't have one (`MixinPreflightTest.aVariableModifierByTypeFailsWhereTheLocalIsOutOfScopeAtItsPoint`). Verified on Forge 47 in extended run 41569833541 (on `8cb2da3`): Balm's hook was left out, with the hook keeping state with it (STATE-1), and the corpus server went past the crop hook to CORPUS-68. On Forge 61 (extended run 41569829683) the hook was left out too, but Balm's 1.21.11 build shares the state with its second hook through MixinExtras' `@Share`, which STATE-1 didn't follow (SHARE-1). Verified there with SHARE-1 (extended run 41658054089, after the sync #258): the corpus server starts and stops normally, and the client gets past `Blocks` and its bootstrap. |
| CORPUS-69 | P0 on Forge 47 with Lithium (the corpus server and client) | `MixinRepairs.superOnlyIntrinsic` (new), `MixinPreflight.checkMerged` | With CORPUS-68 fixed, the Forge 47 corpus server and client stopped as Mixin applied Lithium's `inventory_change_listening.ChestBlockEntityMixin` (extended run 41574441274): "@Intrinsic method must be prefixed interface method, no rename encountered" on `setCachedState` (`m_155250_`). The mixin declares that method only to call the superclass's, so that its `@Inject` at the method's return has a method to inject into; vanilla's chest has none, and Forge 47 patches the chest to override it. Mixin (0.8.5 and 0.8.7) merges an `@Intrinsic` into a method its target has only for an interface method it renamed, and throws on any other that isn't synthetic, whatever the config. | Pre-flight passed every `@Intrinsic` as one Mixin merges, and the corpus stopped earlier (CORPUS-68). | Fix: an `@Intrinsic` (not displacing) of a class mixin that only calls its superclass's method with its own arguments is dropped where every target declares that method, not static nor abstract: the target's method is kept, as `@Intrinsic` keeps a method its target has, and the injector injects into it; nothing is lost, as the dropped method only stood in for the superclass's (an INFO line in the report). Another `@Intrinsic` the target declares (code of its own, or displacing the target's) is reported as Mixin rejecting the mixin, which disables its mod or leaves the mixin out where it can be (`MixinPreflightTest.anIntrinsicOnlyCallingSuperIsDroppedWhereTheTargetDeclaresIt`). Verified on Forge 47 (extended run 41658060068, after the sync #259): with Lithium loaded, and its chest mixin not left out, the corpus server and client got past Lithium's chest and their bootstrap (the server stops later, loading its data packs, on CORPUS-80; the client, baking models, on CORPUS-84). |

ModLauncher's class loader finds a game class only in a module of the game layer that holds its package, and offers one it has no bytes for to the launch plugins, where Mixin generates it; Mixin 0.8.7 (Forge 61 to 66) adds a module of its own holding its generated classes' packages (`mixin_synthetic`), and Forge 47's Mixin 0.8.5 adds none, so the class loader asked its parents, which have no such class. | The test mods use no `@ModifyArgs`, and the corpus stopped earlier (CORPUS-54). | Fix: where the running Mixin's transformation service doesn't add that module itself (no `completeScan` of its own, so Mixin before 0.8.6), Fabricated's adds one to the game layer, holding the two packages and no files (an empty directory under `.fabricated`), as Mixin 0.8.7 does; not when no Fabric mod is loaded, or when a mod file already holds one of the packages, as two modules of a layer can't. A no-op on Forge 61 to 66. Verified on Forge 47 (extended run 41658060068, after the sync #259): the corpus server and client got past `Fluids` and their bootstrap, and the test mod's `@ModifyArgs` runs on Forge 47 (its generated class in a module). |
| LVT-1 | P0 on the Forge 65 server with owo-lib (the corpus server); on Forge 47 to 66 and NeoForge 21.1 and 21.11 for a common mixin with a local of a client class (or any class the side lacks) | `MixinRepairs.withoutUnfoundLocals` (new), `MixinCompat.readsLocalTypes` (new), `PreflightClasses.found` | With ENUM-7 fixed, the Forge 26.2 corpus server stopped as Mixin applied owo's `ui.SlotMixin` (extended run 41578332996): "ClassMetadataNotFoundException: net.minecraft.client.gui.navigation.ScreenRectangle" in "Transform LVT -> var=scissor". owo 0.13.1's common slot mixin keeps a client-only rectangle in a field and its setter (read in its 26.2 source). SpongePowered's Mixin (0.8.5 to 0.8.7, read in their sources) looks up the class of every local in a mixin method's debug table as it merges the method (`MixinTargetContext.transformLVT` through `ClassInfo.forName`) and throws on one it can't find; Fabric's does too before 0.17.0, and from it asks first whether the class is a mixin and looks up nothing else, so on Fabric the mixin applies on a dedicated server. | No test mixin had a local of a class missing on its side, and the corpus stopped earlier (ENUM-7). | Fix: where the running Mixin looks locals up, pre-flight leaves out of the debug tables of a mixin's methods the locals whose class it doesn't find on this side (the game's, the installed jars', the mod's own, the JDK's and libraries'), with an INFO line; the tables are debug information only, and a local of another Fabric mod's class going too costs a debugger its name (`MixinPreflightTest.aLocalOfAClassThisSideLacksLeavesTheDebugTableWhereMixinLooksItUp`). | ENUM-7 | P0 on Forge 61 to 66 with Friends and Foes (the corpus clients' and servers' CORPUS-53); on Forge 47 and NeoForge 21.1 and 21.11 for an enum mixin into an enum NeoForge doesn't make extensible | `ModTranslator.withEnumExtensions`, `EnumValuesMixins` (an enum's values array found by its type), `MixinPreflight.checkMixin` (an enum mixin where Mixin applies none) | Mixin applies enum mixins from Fabric's 0.17.1 only. Where it is older (Forge's 0.8.5 and 0.8.7, NeoForge 21.1's and 21.11's), only an enum mixin into one of NeoForge's extensible enums was translated (ENUM-1, ENUM-5), and only a class tweaker's declared constants were judged (ENUM-6): Friends and Foes' enum mixin into `MobCategory`, which declares its constants with a `@Shadow` constructor and no class tweaker line, went to Forge's Mixin 0.8.7, which threw a `NullPointerException` attaching that constructor ("Attach error for friendsandfoes-fabric.mixins.json:AddCustomSpawnGroupMixin", `MixinPreProcessorStandard.checkMethodNotUnique`), and every Forge 61 to 66 corpus client stopped as `EntityTypes`' class initializer loaded `MobCategory` (extended run 41556865877's 26.2 and 26.3 crash reports; the evidence step printed their innermost cause). | No test had an enum mixin into an enum NeoForge doesn't make extensible where Mixin is older than 0.17.1, and pre-flight judged enum mixins only against NeoForge's extensible enums. Then no test had an enum Forge makes extensible, and the generated mixin ran only merged by hand, without Forge's extender; and the test's enum had no map by name. | Fix: there, an enum mixin becomes an ordinary mixin adding its constants into the enum's class initializer, as ENUM-5's do (Friends and Foes reads its categories with `valueOf`); one that can't (a constant with a body, code using the mixin, the mod's classes reading a constant as a field of the enum) disables its mod with the reason, as Mixin would stop the game whatever the config (`PipelineTest.anEnumMixinBecomesAMixinAddingItsConstantsWhereMixinAppliesNoEnumMixins`, `EnumValuesMixinsTest.theEnumsValuesArrayIsFoundByItsTypeWhereTheGameRenamedIt`). After the sync (extended run 41569829683) the Forge 66 corpus client and server got past Mixin and stopped in Forge's own enum extender as `MobCategory` loaded ("Enum has create method but we could not find $VALUES", `RuntimeEnumExtender`): `MobCategory` is one of the enums Forge makes extensible (`IExtensibleEnum`), whose extender runs after Mixin and finds the values array only while it is still final, and the generated mixin's `@Mutable` shadow of it had made it non-final. Fix: into such an enum the generated mixin makes the constants with the enum's own `create` factories, which the extender writes, and shadows no values array; a constant whose constructor has no factory leaves it an enum mixin, which pre-flight judges (`EnumValuesMixinsTest.anEnumForgeMakesExtensibleGetsItsConstantsFromItsOwnFactories`, which runs it against a factory written as the extender writes one). After the next sync (extended run 41574438102) every Forge 61 to 66 corpus server and client stopped as `MobCategory` loaded, with a `NullPointerException` in `MobCategory.init` ("MobCategory.BY_NAME is null"): each factory registers its constant in the enum's map by name (Forge's `IExtensibleEnum.init`), which the class initializer sets after the values array, and the generated injector ran right after the array. Fix: into an enum Forge makes extensible, the injector calls the factories at the class initializer's end (`TAIL`), as a Forge mod's own call comes after it, with the map set (the test's enum now registers each constant in a map by name, as `MobCategory`'s factory does). Verified on Forge 61 to 66 (extended run 41658054089, after the sync #258): Friends and Foes loads on every corpus server and client, the four servers start and stop normally, the Forge 65 and 66 clients reach their world, and the Forge 61 and 64 clients get past their bootstrap (they stop later, initializing the game, on CORPUS-79). |

| CORPUS-76 | P0 on Forge 47 clients with REI, ShulkerBoxTooltip or Traveler's Backpack (the corpus client); on Forge 47 for any interface mixin that extends its target for injectors alone | `ModTranslator.leaveOutHandlers`, `AccessorShapedMixins` (new) | The corpus client crashed in its loading overlay (extended run 41590139262, again in 41632355968 and 41648278525): `ClassCircularityError: net/minecraft/client/gui/screens/inventory/tooltip/ClientTooltipComponent` as `ModelBakery.init` loaded the interface, and the three mods' entrypoints threw the same. The stack (two definitions in progress, the second asking for `ClientTooltipComponent` again) means the interface was given a supertype that extends it. The cause, read in Mixin's sources (0.8.5 to Fabric's) against the corpus's mixins (Mixin's export found nothing, as the definition failed first): AppleSkin's `TooltipComponentMixin` is an interface mixin of `ClientTooltipComponent` that extends it, for two static injectors. Forge 47's Mixin 0.8.5 applies no injectors in interface mixins, so pre-flight leaves them out (INTERFACE-1), and the mixin was left with no method. Mixin takes an interface mixin whose methods are all accessors and invokers (synthetic ones aside) for an accessor mixin (`MixinInfo.getVariant`), and makes its targets implement the mixin itself, where an ordinary interface mixin gives them its interfaces less the target: `ClientTooltipComponent` was made to implement an interface extending it. Fabric API's `TooltipComponentMixin` became an accessor mixin the same way, harmlessly, as it extends nothing. | No test left out every method of an interface mixin. | Fixed in core: an interface mixin that leaving out methods turns into an accessor mixin is left out whole where its config lists it and nothing is left for its targets (no accessor, and no interface but its targets); otherwise it no longer names its targets among its interfaces (`AccessorShapedMixins`; `PipelineTest.anInterfaceMixinLeftWithoutItsInjectorsNoLongerMakesItsTargetItsOwnSupertype` fails without it). AppleSkin's and Fabric API's tooltip hooks stay left out on Forge 47 (INTERFACE-1). Verified on Forge 47 after the sync (#259): the corpus client's model loading and REI's, ShulkerBoxTooltip's and Traveler's Backpack's entrypoints no longer throw it (extended run 41658060068). The client now stops later, baking its models (CORPUS-84). |

| CORPUS-74 | P0 on Forge 61 to 66 clients with MoreCulling, and Forge 47's with owo-lib (the corpus clients); on Forge for any mod shadowing a field another mod's mixin adds | `MixinRepairs.sharedFieldRepair` (new), `MixinCompat.shadowsMixinFields` (new), `MixinPreflight.checkMembersOf` | The Forge 26.2 corpus client crashed as Mixin applied MoreCulling's `BlockModelRenderState_fabricCullMixin` (extended run 41590136017): "@Shadow field mesh was not located in the target class net.minecraft.client.renderer.block.BlockModelRenderState", the field Fabric API's renderer mixin adds there. The Forge 47 corpus client crashed the same way on owo's creative screen mixin, which shadows Fabric API's `fabric_currentPage` (extended run 41590139262). Fabric's Mixin finds a `@Shadow` field among the fields of the mixins it prepared before (`TargetClassContext`'s `mixinFields`, from 0.12.0, read in its sources); SpongePowered's, which Forge runs (0.8.5 and 0.8.7), looks in the target's own fields only. Pre-flight took such a shadow as fine wherever an installed mixin adds the field. | No test mod shadowed a field another mixin adds, and the corpus clients stopped earlier. | Fixed in core: where the running Mixin finds only the target's own fields, such a shadow is declared as the other mixin declares the field (its access and signature, Mixin's annotations off), and Mixin, which adds a mixin's new field only where its target has none of that name and descriptor yet, merges the two into one, used by both mods' code as on Fabric; one that can't be declared so (a static shadow of an instance field, a writable one of a final field, an alias) fails pre-flight there (`MixinPreflightTest.aShadowOfAFieldAnotherModsMixinAddsIsThatFieldWhereMixinFindsOnlyTheTargetsOwn`). The Forge test mods count the server's ticks in one mixin's field and read it through another's shadow, which passes on all five Forge versions. Verified in the corpus: MoreCulling 1.8.1 loads on the Forge 65 client, which reaches the world, and Forge 47's client passes owo's shadow (extended runs 41632339327 and 41632355968). |
| CORPUS-71 | P0 on Forge 61 to 66 clients with GeckoLib (the corpus clients) | `NamespacedWrapperEarlyWriteMixin` (new, `forge`) | Forge locks the vanilla registries it manages once it has taken its vanilla snapshot. GeckoLib's constants, loaded on a client as the client bootstrap builds the special model renderers, register its data components from their static initializer, which works on Fabric, and the registry refused the write. | The test mods register in their entrypoints only. | Fixed on `forge` (#254): until the main entrypoints have run, a write to a locked registry opens it for that write and leaves it as it was, as NeoForge's `MappedRegistryEarlyWriteMixin` does. | CORPUS-75 | P0 on Forge 64 and 65 with Waystones (the corpus servers) | `NamespacedWrapperAliasMixin` (new, both Forge branches) | The 26.1.2 and 26.2 corpus servers stopped loading data packs (extended run 41590136017): "Unknown registry key in ResourceKey[minecraft:root / minecraft:block]: waystones:mossy_waystone" in Waystones' world generation, which names its renamed waystones by their old names. Waystones keeps them as aliases with Fabric API's `FabricRegistry.addAlias`, which Fabric API implements in `MappedRegistry` and resolves where its lookups begin; Forge's `NamespacedWrapper` answers those lookups itself, from its holders and Forge's registry, so the aliases were kept where nothing looked. | No test mod added an alias. | Forge's registries take `addAlias` with Fabric API's checks and its invariant (an alias names an entry, never another alias), add the alias to Forge's registry too (whose own lookups, snapshots and sync to clients then know it), and resolve it where the wrapper's lookups by name and key begin (#254, #249). The test mod registers an item under a new name with its old one as an alias, and the server run with Fabric API checks both lookups and a tag naming it by its old name (all five; PR runs 41592391853 and 41592399896). Verified in the corpus: the Forge 64 and 65 corpus servers load Waystones' data pack and start (extended run 41594474962, #254's c5ced02). |
| CORPUS-73 | P0 on Forge 47 to 66 with a Fabric mod making its objects before registering what they hold (TechReborn's fluids on Forge 47, 64 and 65; Lootr's block items on Forge 47: the corpus servers) | `BucketItemEarlyFluidMixin` (both Forge branches), `LiquidBlockEarlyFluidMixin`, `RecordItemEarlySoundMixin` and `BlockItemEarlyBlockMixin` (`forge-legacy`) | Fabric lets a mod make its objects first and register them after, in any order. | NET-10 | P1 for every Cardinal Components API mod on NeoForge (component sync: Trinkets on the 1.21.1 corpus client) | `MeasuredPackets`, `GenericPacketSplitterMeasureMixin`, `PacketEncoderMeasuredMixin` (new; NeoForge's `GenericPacketSplitter`) | The 1.21.1 corpus client logged "Failed to process a synchronized task of the payload: cardinal-components:entity_sync" 19 times (extended run 41512581949): Trinkets' `applySyncPacket` read past the end of an empty buffer (`EOFException` in `FriendlyByteBuf.readNbt`). NeoForge's packet splitter encodes each outgoing packet to measure it, then hands the packet itself to vanilla's encoder, which encodes it again (read in NeoForge's 21.1 and 26.3 sources; singleplayer's connection has the splitter too). Cardinal Components API writes a sync payload's buffer with `writeBytes(ByteBuf)` (`MorePacketCodecs.REG_BYTE_BUF`, read in its 6.1.3 and current sources), which reads the buffer to its end, so the second encoding sent an empty one. Fabric encodes a packet once. Every such mod's component sync failed so on NeoForge, in singleplayer and on a server with NeoForge clients. | No test payload's codec emptied what it wrote: the test mods' payloads wrote plain fields, which a second encoding writes the same. | `01c9258`: for a packet carrying a Fabric payload, the splitter keeps the bytes it measured and vanilla's encoder writes them in place of a second encoding, so the payload's codec runs once, as on Fabric; other packets are coded as NeoForge codes them. The test mods send a payload with Cardinal Components API's codec shape (`NetworkingTest.BufferPing`), which every client checks arrives whole ("payload encoded once"). With Forgified Fabric API providing networking the fix stands down with Fabricated's module, and the check reports what arrived as skipped. Verified in extended run 41527263549 (on `7cc24be`): the check passed in three client runs on each of the five versions, and the 1.21.1 corpus client logged no entity sync failure. |

Gson's module doesn't export `com.google.gson.internal`, so the game's module class loader maps no module to it and hands the class to its fallback, which ModLauncher makes its boot layer's loader, holding the launcher's libraries (read in its `ModuleLayerHandler`). LOADER-2's loader for the jars mods add to the class path replaced that fallback and went to the game loader's parent instead, which has no Gson. Since LOADER-2 (v0.4.0), on NeoForge 21.1 only: FML 10 to 12 make the fallback the loader's parent (read in their `FMLLoader`). The package guard, which the owner suspected, wasn't involved: it already leaves a Fabric jar's copy of a platform package out for every layer the game reads, unexported packages included, and the pack's report shows no Gson package taken. | No test loaded a class only the fallback serves: a new world has no stats file to read, and OneConfig's read only logged a warning, in the informational Polyfrost run. | The loader goes in front of the fallback each platform names (`Fml.gameFallback`: ModLauncher's boot layer loader on NeoForge 21.1, the loader's parent on FML 10 and later). | SUPER-1 | P0 on Forge 64 to 66 clients with Fabric API | `DetachedSupers`, `MixinCompat.rebindsInterfaceSuperCalls` (core; applied where SpongePowered's Mixin runs: Forge 61 to 66 and Forge 47) | Past NET-8, a Forge 64 to 66 client with the official Fabric API jar crashed as it drew the first block: "Method 'void SimpleModelWrapper.emitQuads(QuadEmitter, Predicate)' must be Methodref constant" (run 40431467223). Fabric API's renderer gives `SimpleModelWrapper`, a record, a mixin extending `Object` that calls `BlockStateModelPart.super.emitQuads`. Mixin calls such a mixin's super "detached" and rebinds each of its `super` calls to the class in the target's hierarchy that implements the method; for an interface's default, found through the target's own interfaces (Fabricated adds Fabric's injected interfaces to the classes themselves), that is the target, and the call keeps its interface reference, which the JVM refuses. SpongePowered's 0.8.7 does this (`MixinTargetContext.updateStaticBinding`, read in its source); Fabric's Mixin stopped rebinding calls to interfaces in 0.15.0 (FabricMC/Mixin#150), so NeoForge, whose renderer module is native anyway, is unaffected. | No Forge client ran with the Fabric API jar. | `cd3e633`: where the running Mixin is one of SpongePowered's releases, a kept mixin whose superclass isn't its targets' one and that calls an interface's method as its super gets its targets' superclass (in its declaration, generic signature and constructors' super call, found as Mixin finds it), so Mixin keeps its calls as they are; a mixin that also calls its own superclass's methods as its super keeps it. `DetachedSupersTest` covers it. In runs 40433805134 and 40433807263 it changes Fabric API's `SimpleModelWrapperMixin` on Forge 64 to 66 and nothing on Forge 61 or 47, and the client with the Fabric API jar reaches a world on all five, every check passing. |

Orderings outside a cycle, and those after NeoForge or Forge mods, are unchanged; the cache key already holds the orderings, so cached overlays are rebuilt. `LoadOrderingsTest` (pairs, rings, linked rings, a diamond, a tail into a cycle whichever way its id sorts, a 200,000-mod chain) and `PipelineTest` (a pair, a ring and a dependent, on NeoForge's and Forge's descriptors: no cycle, everything else kept) pass, and each platform's server smoke now loads two Fabric mods that depend on each other and must start. | PLAIN-1 | P0 on Forge 47 | `MappingComposer.composeSrg`, `MappingTable.mapPlainMethod`, `IntermediaryRemapper`, `JarRemapper` (any Fabric mod on Forge 47) | With the official Fabric API jar on Forge 47 (forge-legacy's run on #113, past ALLOW-1), the server stopped once mods had loaded: "AbstractMethodError: Receiver class ...ModResourcePackCreator$$Lambda does not define or inherit an implementation of the resolved method 'abstract ... m_247679_(java.lang.String)' of interface ...Pack$ResourcesSupplier". Intermediary leaves the methods the official jar keeps unobfuscated under their names (a functional interface's `open`, `createMenu`, `accept`, `codec`, `bake`) and lists no mapping for them, so Fabric mods use those names; SRG renames 230 of them on 1.20.1 (Intermediary's and MCPConfig's files, compared). A lambda takes the name of its functional interface's method, which ASM hands the remapper without the interface: a class remapper reads it from `LambdaMetafactory`'s arguments. A refmap value or annotation string naming such a method with its owner and descriptor (`Lowner;name(desc)`, as an `@At` target gives it) takes SRG's name too: Fabric API's transfer refmap names `DispenseItemBehavior.dispense` (SRG's `m_6115_`) for `DropperBlockMixin`'s `@At`, which Forge 47's pre-flight left out on #113 as no call goes by that name. A selector giving the bare name stays as it is; none of Fabric API 0.92.12's refmaps has one. A name Intermediary renames elsewhere is joined where the descriptor differs: the table left out every official name Intermediary renames anywhere, and three unrelated `get(F)` methods it renames kept `IoSupplier.get()` (SRG's `m_247737_`) out, which stopped Forge 47's server in Fabric API's resource pack on #116 (NoSuchMethodError); an override of a renamed method shares its name and descriptor, so it stays out. That adds 77 methods on 1.20.1, mostly functional interfaces' `create`, `apply`, `test` and `get` (`BlockEntityType.BlockEntitySupplier.create`, `MenuType.MenuSupplier.create`, `EntityType.EntityFactory.create`); Fabric API 0.92.12 calls, implements or makes lambdas of 17 of them, and none is left unmapped. `RemapTest.joinsTheMethodsIntermediaryLeavesUnderTheirOfficialNamesToSrgs`, `RemapTest.aRefmapNamesAMethodIntermediaryLeavesUnderItsOfficialNameBySrgs`, `RemapTest.anOfficialNameIntermediaryRenamesWithAnotherDescriptorIsJoinedToo`. |

Pre-flight took a point found in any target method as enough, while Mixin counts the injections in all of them against `require`. | Tests had one target method per injector. | This pass: where `require` is above one and every target is known, pre-flight counts the injections in every target method (each matching instruction in the slice, one with an ordinal) and reports the shortfall, with the methods that lost the point; LEAVE-1 then leaves out FrozenLib's injector instead of the game stopping. `MixinPreflightTest.requireCountsInjectionsInEveryTargetMethod`. |
| LAMBDA-2 | P1 on 1.21.11 | `MixinPreflight.lambdaCallRepairs` | REQ-1 leaves FrozenLib's biome registry hook out on 1.21.11, where NeoForge's build runs `RegistryDataLoader.loadElementFromResource`'s register call from an `Optional` callback (a lambda of the method). | Pre-flight only renamed lambdas the game numbers differently. | This pass: a call-site injector (`@WrapOperation`, `@ModifyExpressionValue`, `@Redirect`, `@ModifyArg(s)`, `@WrapWithCondition`) whose call or field access is in none of a target method's instructions but in exactly one lambda the method creates gets that lambda added to its methods. Its handler takes the instruction's own operands, the same there; one taking MixinExtras sugar, not static where the lambda is, or with an ordinal or a slice, isn't moved. `MixinPreflightTest.aCallNeoForgeMovedIntoALambdaOfTheMethodIsFollowedThere`. |

It looks the storage up by reflection and, when that fails, logs it and keeps its entrypoints for its own helper, which then runs them. | The linkage warnings named the classes, but a warning doesn't stop a mixin plugin from running. | This pass: the three classes exist, without an instance mods can reach (`FabricLoaderImpl` has no storage field, no container is a `ModContainerImpl`), so FrozenLib's lookup fails as it plans for. `LinkageCheckerTest.fabricLoaderReferencesAreCheckedAgainstFabricatedsOwn` covers a class implementing `EntrypointMetadata`. | AT-1 | P0 on 1.21.11 | `AccessTransformerConverter` | The 1.21.11 advanced corpus server stopped while FML loaded the access transformers (run on `fad7762`): "Invalid AT final conflicts" for `InstrumentItem.getInstrument`, one mod's file making it final (Fabric's implicit final for a private instance method made accessible) and another's removing it (extendable). Fabric merges all mods' wideners first, where extendable wins; NeoForge reads each mod's file on its own and refuses the conflict. | Merging was tested within one mod's file only. | This pass: that implicit final isn't written. Fabricated opened the root only for `FabricRegistryBuilder` during registration events. With the root opened (run on `b0f3606`), Wover's next write stopped it: "... worldgen/structure_type / wover:random_nbt_structure". | No test wrote to a built-in registry through vanilla's own code, or before NeoForge's registration events. | This pass: a frozen built-in registry receiving a write before NeoForge's registration has ended is opened for it: the root until common setup, closed again with the root `FabricRegistryBuilder` opens; any other registry only before NeoForge's registration events, which then open, run over and freeze every registry in the root themselves (read in NeoForge's `CommonModLoader`, `RegistryManager` and `GameData`). Server checks: "late registry OK" (a registry added from the main entrypoint) and "early registry writes OK" (a vanilla registry entry and a registry added from `RegistryDataLoader`'s class initializer, by a test mod mixin). Not built locally (mc modules build in CI only). |
| REG-4 | P0 on 26.3.0.95 | `NeoForgeRegistryInternals.alsoInLoaderList` (Fabric data-pack registries with Wover installed: FrozenLib, Lithostitched) | Pinning NeoForge 26.3.0.95 (PR #67), the 26.3 advanced corpus server and client stopped loading registries: 261 Wilder Wild features failed with "Registry does not exist: frozenlib:config_predicate_provider", and 3 Lithostitched density functions failed to parse. Since NeoForge's #3659 (2026-10-17, read in its `26.3.x` branch) NeoForge loads data-pack registries from `RegistryDataLoader.WORLD_REGISTRIES`, which it keeps as a view of its own list, instead of from that list. Wover replaces the field with a copy at the end of the class's initializer (its `RegistryDataLoaderMixin`, read in its `release/26.3` branch), so registries Fabricated adds to NeoForge's list afterwards, as Fabric's `DynamicRegistries` asks, never reached the loader. | The test mod's own dynamic registry had no mod replacing the list beside it; the corpus step is informational. | This pass: when NeoForge loads from the loader's lists (it captures them) and a mod replaced one, Fabricated adds what NeoForge's list has and the replacement lacks: whenever a Fabric registry is added, and once mods have loaded, since NeoForge adds its own data-pack registries after the loader's class initializer too (with only Fabric's added, the 26.3 servers stopped at "Missing registry: neoforge:biome_modifier", run on `59c66e2`). Each addition is logged. The test mod's mixin plugin replaces `WORLD_REGISTRIES` with a copy as Wover does, where the class has it, so the server gate covers both (its server stopped on `59c66e2` too). On `2b15331` (PR #67) every 26.3 gate passes with the test mod's copy in place, and the advanced corpus server starts with no registry error left. |

A constant with a body overriding the one method the enum forwards to a delegate (`GrassColorModifier`'s `ColorModifier`) is made with the constructor taking the delegate and a class generated next to the mod's mixins that runs the body. Only straight-line code using nothing of the mixin moves, and nothing when a class of the mod reads a constant as a field of the enum, which the mixin can't add; the mod is disabled then, as before. `EnumValuesMixinsTest` builds such an enum and mixin, merges them as Mixin would, and runs the class initializer. The handler stays a private method. A bridge takes the call's exact types, so a handler whose receiver is a `@Coerce`d supertype isn't bridged (the bridge wouldn't carry the annotation, and Mixin would refuse it); generated methods get names the mixin doesn't use yet, as handlers may share a name; and the former handler is `@Unique`, so Mixin renames it rather than merging it over another mixin's method of the same name (all three found by this pass's review). | LIB-1 | P1 | `LibraryBridges`, `ModTranslator.withLibraryBridges` (Carpet, any mod giving a library class its interfaces) | Carpet's mixins into Brigadier's `CommandNode` and `CommandDispatcher` only give them its interfaces `CommandNodeInterface` and `CommandDispatcherInterface`, implemented with their private maps, to remove a script's commands (read in its `CommandNode_scarpetCommandsMixin`, `CommandDispatcher_scarpetCommandsMixin` and `script.external.Vanilla`). The mixin's methods become static methods of that class, taking the library object first; its shadowed fields are read and written through `java.lang.reflect.Field`, which works as Brigadier is in the unnamed module (its stack frames carry no module name in the runs' logs). The mod's casts to the interfaces, `instanceof` checks and calls of their methods, method references included, call the class. | NET-7 | P1 | `GenericPacketSplitterContextMixin`, `ServerLoginPacketListenerImplMixin`, `client.ClientHandshakePacketContextMixin`, `client.ClientPacketListenerContextMixin` (Polymer on 26.x, any codec reading Fabric's packet context) | With NET-6, the 26.2 and 26.3 advanced clients joined their worlds, then lost the connection (run on `9b2e532`). NeoForge's packet splitter encodes each packet before vanilla's encoder to measure it, and decodes the packets it reassembles; neither ran with the context bound, so Polymer's ingredient codec failed on Fabric's recipe sync ("PacketContext.get() is null"). Fabricated also never put into the context what Fabric API puts there (upstream's login, handshake and configuration listener mixins): the server and its registries, the player's profile, the client's registries. Polymer warned that chunk packets lacked the registries, and its item codecs fell back to a lookup of their own. | The test mod sends a 3 MiB payload whose codec checks the context while the server encodes it and the client decodes it, which the client smoke test checks on 26.x. | This pass: the splitter codes with the connection's context bound, and the context holds the server and its registries from the login listener's creation, the profile from login, and the client's registries from the play listener's creation. Also: `PacketContext`'s constants were `PacketContextImpl`'s keys, and initializing a class first initializes the interfaces with default methods it implements. When the impl class initialized first (a connection's first context), it initialized `PacketContext` before its own keys were set, so its constants stayed null: every lookup through `PacketContext.REGISTRY_ACCESS` and the others found nothing, `CONNECTION` included. The runs where a mod touched `PacketContext` first passed; the test mods alone and the recipe viewers didn't (CI on `27caea2` and `38c4ce3`: "registries false, profile false, server false, connection null"; making the keys without `PacketContext.key`, on `1c3710c`, didn't change that, and a standalone class pair shows the rule). The keys now live in `PacketContextKeys`, which both read, so either order works. |

Verified: `NativeModulesTest`, `PipelineTest.aNativeModuleAnotherModProvidesIsThatModsOnly`; server runs with `ci/neoforge/standins.py`'s mods on 1.21.1, 1.21.11 and 26.1.2, every check passing (the test mods' among them, on the provider's copies). On Forge 47 and 61 to 66 (task 3, #240 and #241): the same policy, from core's platform-neutral parts, for Forge's native modules (all six of Forge 47's are ones Forgified Fabric API for 1.20.1 provides), their mixins and their bridges; a server run per Forge version with stand-ins is a gate. |

On NeoForge 21.1 the game's layer reads both and resolution fails, with or without Fabric mods (reproduced with a stand-in). On FML 10 and later plugin libraries load with a plain class loader, so nothing fails to resolve, but the copy's own classes and its language loader service still ran. | As PROVIDER-1. | The owner's policy, "always ours": an FML mod file reader ahead of FML's own reads any jar but a Fabricated build without `net/fabricmc/loader/`, `net/fabricmc/api/` and the service files naming their classes: filtered contents on FML 4, a copy without them in `.fabricated/loader-api/` on FML 10 and later (which can't filter a plugin library), of the same type. A first version made such a jar a game library on FML 10 and later; with a stale Forge build of Fabricated beside the NeoForge one (a local run), that put its copies of Fabricated's classes in the game's layer, ahead of the real ones, and the 1.21.11 stand-in run stopped. On Forge (task 3, #240 and #241, the owner's decision of 2026-11-02): Forge has no reader that can leave another jar's copy out, and beside Fabricated's, Forge 47 stopped building the game's layer with "ResolutionException: Modules net.fabricmc.loader and io.github.cmdralph.fabricated export package net.fabricmc.loader.launch.common", naming neither mod (the diagnostic run of Forgified Fabric API for 1.20.1, 41371266132). The mod locator refuses a Forge jar with its own copy, or nesting one (`FabricLoaderCopies`, bounded in depth and entries), at once, naming the jar and the nested one (`ConflictingMods$LaunchRefused`, which Forge's discovery doesn't collect, so Forge doesn't go on to an error screen that would fail the same way). A refusal server per Forge version with a stand-in nesting Fabricated's own classes is a gate. Coexisting is task 5. |

Defects in this session's own new code, found by CI before merge: the renderer API mixin first hit NeoForge's
deprecated 4-argument `SectionCompiler.compile`. It now targets the 5-argument overload by descriptor (`0dbdfda`,
crash in run 229).

## Closed without a change in Fabricated

Findings that are another mod's own behaviour (the same on Fabric), a known limit listed in `COMPATIBILITY.md`, or
not worth a change, each with the evidence that closed it.

| ID | Sev | Location | Issue | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CORPUS-114 | — (Polymer's, not Fabricated's) | Polymer 0.18.7's ingredient stream codecs (`polymer-core`'s `IngredientMixin`, `IngredientExtension.BaseStreamCodec` and `OptionalStreamCodec`, on with its default `extendedRecipeIngredients`); the Forge test mods' ingredient network checks (`ForgeIngredientCodecTest`) | Past LIB-3, a local Forge 66 advanced corpus server failed the test mods' checks that an empty optional ingredient and a Fabric mod's own ingredient go over the network: "Cannot invoke PacketContext.get(ReadKey) because the return value of PacketContext.get() is null". The check of Fabric API's custom ingredients, which that run doesn't count, logged the same. They pass without the corpus; a Forge 66 server with Polymer alone beside Fabric API and the test mods fails the same two. Read in Polymer's classes: it wraps both of `Ingredient`'s stream codecs where the class makes them, and its writer starts with `PacketContext.get().get(PacketContext.CONNECTION)`, without checking for a context. Fabric API binds the context only while a packet is coded (its `PacketEncoderMixin` and `PacketDecoderMixin`, a scoped value; read in fabric-networking-api-v1 6.3.10, whose `PacketContext.get()` is null outside), and the checks write their buffer at the first server tick, outside any packet: on Fabric Polymer's writer throws there too. In a connection the context is bound, which the 26.x clients' `packet-context-encoding` check covers (NET-6, NET-7). | Not Fabricated's: the checks stay as they are. Whether they should write inside a packet's context, as a connection does, so that a mod's codec needing one doesn't fail them, is the owner's call. |
| CORPUS-113 | — (Wover's, not Fabricated's) | Wover's biome tags (`BiomeManagerImpl.onBootstrapTags`, `CustomBootstrapContext`), the 1.21.1 advanced corpus server | The advanced corpus server's `/reload` fails ("Failed to execute reload", a `NullPointerException`: `BiomeBootstrapContextImpl.prepareTags` on a null `context`), so the test mod's check that its reload listener runs again misses (extended runs 42524794672 and 42527530813). Read in Wover's source (MIT, its 1.21 branch): its tag loader mixin asks for the biome tags' additions each time the tags load, and its biome module's handler takes the context the biome registry's bootstrap made, then drops it ("preparing tags is the last step"). A `/reload` loads the tags again without loading the worldgen registries, so the handler finds no context, on Fabric as here. The server keeps its data as it was. The fourth round's failure is the same, reproduced on a local NeoForge 1.21.1 advanced server with WorldWeaver 21.0.13 (`BiomeManagerImpl.onBootstrapTags`, line 95): read in its classes, the handler takes the context through `initContext(null)` and invalidates it after `prepareTags`, so a second load gets null. | Not Fabricated's: the check stays as it is, and passes on every other server run. |

Wilder Wild ships a reinforced deepslate blockstate with an `axis` property, for the pillar block it makes of it when `newReinforcedDeepslate` is on (the default). | CORPUS-27 | — | FrozenLib's config (advanced corpus, every 26.x version) | FrozenLib logs "Unable to read config entry" for Wilder Wild's entries (`newReinforcedDeepslate`, `unpassableRail`, `reachBoostBeacon` and others; 28 a run on 26.1.2, CI of PR #127). Read in FrozenLib's `ConfigSerializer` and `ConfigData`: an entry is read from the config file's map, and an entry absent from it fails to parse and logs this; on a fresh config directory, as in every CI run, the map is empty, so each entry read before FrozenLib first saves the file logs it, and keeps its default. | FrozenLib's first-run behaviour, as on Fabric (by its source; not run there): left as it is. |
| CORPUS-26 | — | Translated mods' packs on 1.21.11 (First-person Model, its TRansition library) | Vanilla logs "Couldn't load mod/firstperson pack metadata: Pack declares support for format 75, but game versions supporting formats 17 to 81 require a supported_formats field" (8 to 12 times a run, client and server; CI of PR #91). NeoForge loads the pack anyway: a mod's pack whose metadata doesn't read is compatible to its `ResourcePackLoader.readMeta` (read in its 1.21.11 and 26.3 source). Fabric reads no mod's own pack metadata for its grouped mod resources, so the error is NeoForge's alone. Lootr's built-in `old_textures` pack logs the same family ("missing mandatory fields min_format and max_format"); a built-in pack's metadata is read on Fabric too, so whether it fails there is UNVERIFIED. | Noise, nothing lost: left as it is. |
